## 30. Appendix B: PYQ Question Bank (Subject-wise)

> This appendix is a large, structured bank of PYQ-style questions (Prelims and Mains format, paraphrased/illustrative based on actual recurring UPSC patterns) organized subject-wise, each with a concise model answer/explanation. Use this bank as a **self-test tool** — attempt each question first, then check the explanation, rather than passively reading through it.

### B.1 History — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** Consider the following pairs: Site — Associated Civilization/Period. Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched?
*Answer approach:* Cross-check each site (e.g., Lothal-Harappan, Rakhigarhi-Harappan, Hastinapur-later Vedic) against its verified archaeological period; match systematically rather than relying on partial memory.

**Q2 (Prelims).** With reference to the ancient Sangam literature, which of the following statements is/are correct?
*Answer approach:* Recall Sangam literature's geographic origin (Tamil region), thematic content (society, trade, kingship of Chera/Chola/Pandya), and time period (roughly 3rd century BCE–3rd century CE).

**Q3 (Prelims).** Consider the following Buddhist/Jain councils: which was held under whose patronage, and where?
*Answer approach:* Build a simple table — Council number, ruler/patron, location, key outcome (e.g., compilation of texts, schism) — and memorize as a single unit rather than isolated facts.

**Q4 (Prelims).** With reference to the Bhakti movement, consider the following statements about its key proponents and their regions.
*Answer approach:* Map each Bhakti saint to their region and core philosophy (e.g., Kabir - Nirguna, Chaitanya - Bengal Vaishnavism, Basava - Lingayat movement in Karnataka).

**Q5 (Prelims).** Which Governor-General/Viceroy is associated with a given administrative/legislative reform?
*Answer approach:* Use your master timeline (Section 8.4) — e.g., Lord Curzon-Partition of Bengal 1905, Lord Ripon-Local Self Government 1882, Lord Mountbatten-Partition/Independence 1947.

**Q6 (Prelims).** Consider the following events of the Indian freedom struggle — arrange them in chronological order.
*Answer approach:* Anchor to a few fixed reference points (1857 Revolt, 1885 INC formation, 1905 Bengal Partition, 1919 Jallianwala Bagh, 1930 Dandi March, 1942 Quit India, 1947 Independence) and place other events relative to these anchors.

**Q7 (Prelims).** With reference to the Government of India Act 1935, which of the following provisions is/are correct?
*Answer approach:* Recall its key features — provincial autonomy, federation of provinces & princely states (never fully implemented), dyarchy at the centre, expanded franchise; distinguish from the 1919 Act's dyarchy at the provincial level.

**Q8 (Prelims).** Consider the following classical dance forms and their states of origin: how many pairs are correctly matched?
*Answer approach:* Bharatanatyam-Tamil Nadu, Kathak-Uttar Pradesh, Kathakali/Mohiniyattam-Kerala, Odissi-Odisha, Kuchipudi-Andhra Pradesh, Manipuri-Manipur, Sattriya-Assam.

**Q9 (Mains, GS1, 15 marks).** "The socio-religious reform movements of the 19th century laid the ideological foundation for the Indian freedom struggle." Discuss.
*Model answer approach:* Introduction — define the socio-religious reform context (Brahmo Samaj, Arya Samaj, Aligarh Movement etc.). Body — (a) challenged regressive social practices (sati, caste rigidity), building a rationalist ethos; (b) promoted education and vernacular press, expanding political consciousness; (c) instilled pride in Indian civilizational heritage, countering colonial cultural denigration; (d) some limitations — reforms were often elite-driven, uneven regional spread. Conclusion — these movements collectively created the intellectual and organizational scaffolding later used by the political freedom struggle.

**Q10 (Mains, GS1, 10 marks).** Discuss the salient features of the Harappan urban planning system.
*Model answer approach:* Grid-pattern streets, standardized burnt-brick construction, advanced drainage systems, granaries, the Great Bath at Mohenjo-daro, absence of clear monumental temples/palaces (debated), standardized weights/measures suggesting trade regulation.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. Which ruler is credited with convening the Fourth Buddhist Council? (Kanishka)
12. The term "Ashtapradhan" is associated with which polity? (Maratha administration under Shivaji)
13. Which newspaper was edited by Bal Gangadhar Tilak? (Kesari and Mahratta)
14. The Poona Pact (1932) was signed between which two parties? (Gandhi and Ambedkar, regarding separate electorates for Depressed Classes)
15. Who founded the Indian National Army's second phase leadership after Rash Behari Bose? (Subhas Chandra Bose)
16. The Rowlatt Act (1919) is associated with which repressive provision? (Arrest and detention without trial)
17. Which session of the Indian National Congress first raised the demand for "Purna Swaraj"? (Lahore Session, 1929)
18. The Cripps Mission (1942) was sent to India in the context of which global event? (World War II, seeking Indian cooperation)
19. Which Act introduced separate electorates for Muslims for the first time? (Indian Councils Act / Morley-Minto Reforms, 1909)
20. The "Doctrine of Lapse" is associated with which Governor-General? (Lord Dalhousie)

### B.2 Geography — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements regarding the Himalayan mountain system: which is/are correct regarding its formation (plate tectonics — collision of Indian and Eurasian plates)?
*Answer approach:* Understand the mechanism (continental-continental collision, ongoing uplift, seismicity) rather than memorizing static facts alone — this mechanism-based understanding resolves many variant-phrased questions.

**Q2 (Prelims).** With reference to Indian rivers, which of the following originates from a glacier versus a monsoon-fed source?
*Answer approach:* Classify Himalayan rivers (glacier + snowmelt + monsoon fed, perennial) versus Peninsular rivers (monsoon-fed only, seasonal) — this single distinction resolves most river-origin classification questions.

**Q3 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the Indian monsoon mechanism — which is/are correct (ITCZ shift, Tibetan Plateau heating, jet stream role)?
*Answer approach:* Build a mental flow: differential heating of land/sea → low pressure over land → ITCZ shifts northward → monsoon winds draw moisture from Indian Ocean → orographic rainfall on Western Ghats/Himalayan foothills.

**Q4 (Prelims).** Which type of soil is best suited for cotton cultivation, and where is it predominantly found?
*Answer approach:* Regur/black soil (derived from Deccan Trap basalt), predominantly Maharashtra, Madhya Pradesh, Gujarat — high moisture retention, ideal for cotton.

**Q5 (Prelims).** Consider the following pairs: Mineral — State (leading producer). How many pairs are correctly matched?
*Answer approach:* Build a mineral-belt master table — iron ore (Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand), coal (Jharkhand, Odisha, Chhattisgarh), bauxite (Odisha), mica (Jharkhand, Andhra Pradesh).

**Q6 (Prelims).** With reference to ocean currents, consider the following statements about the Gulf Stream and its effect on climate.
*Answer approach:* Warm current along the eastern coast of North America extending into the North Atlantic, moderating the climate of Western Europe — understand the general principle that warm currents raise coastal temperatures, cold currents lower them.

**Q7 (Prelims).** Which of the following passes connects a given pair of regions across the Himalayas?
*Answer approach:* Build a passes-master-list (Nathu La-Sikkim/Tibet, Zoji La-Kashmir/Ladakh, Shipki La-Himachal/Tibet) tagged to current-affairs relevance (trade routes, border significance).

**Q8 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about El Niño and La Niña — which correctly describes their impact on the Indian monsoon?
*Answer approach:* El Niño (warming of central-eastern Pacific) generally correlates with weaker/deficient Indian monsoon; La Niña (cooling) generally correlates with a stronger/above-normal monsoon — though correlation isn't absolute.

**Q9 (Mains, GS1, 15 marks).** Discuss the factors influencing the distribution of the iron and steel industry in India.
*Model answer approach:* Introduction — define locational factors framework. Body — (a) raw material proximity (iron ore, coal in the Chotanagpur plateau region), (b) power/energy availability, (c) transport/rail connectivity, (d) water availability for processing, (e) historical/colonial-era establishment patterns, (f) government policy/investment. Conclusion — note the emerging shift toward coastal/port-based steel plants due to import dependency and export orientation.

**Q10 (Mains, GS1, 10 marks).** Examine the geomorphological factors responsible for frequent landslides in the Himalayan region.
*Model answer approach:* Young, tectonically active fold mountains with unconsolidated sediment; steep slopes; high monsoon rainfall intensity; deforestation and unplanned construction exacerbating natural fragility; seismic activity triggering slope failure.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. Which is the only cold desert in India mentioned frequently in Geography questions? (Ladakh, in the trans-Himalayan region)
12. The Chilika Lake, India's largest coastal lagoon, is located in which state? (Odisha)
13. Which strait separates India from Sri Lanka? (Palk Strait, along with the Gulf of Mannar)
14. The Sundarbans mangrove ecosystem is formed at the delta of which rivers? (Ganga-Brahmaputra-Meghna delta)
15. Which Indian state has the longest coastline? (Gujarat)
16. The Deccan Traps, a large igneous province, are primarily associated with which type of rock? (Basalt, formed via volcanic activity)
17. Which wind system is responsible for the "Mango Shower" in Kerala/Karnataka? (Pre-monsoon showers, sometimes linked to local convectional patterns)
18. The Great Rann of Kutch is a distinctive geographical feature of which state? (Gujarat)
19. Which is the southernmost point of mainland India? (Kanyakumari — note Indira Point in the Andaman & Nicobar Islands is the southernmost point of the entire Indian Union territory)
20. The Western Disturbances that bring winter rain to North India originate from which region? (The Mediterranean Sea region, travelling eastward)

### B.3 Polity — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** With reference to the Fundamental Rights, consider the following statements: which Articles are suspended automatically during a National Emergency, and which require a separate Presidential Order?
*Answer approach:* Article 19 rights are automatically suspended during a National Emergency declared on grounds of war/external aggression (not on grounds of armed rebellion); Articles 20 and 21 can NEVER be suspended even during Emergency (post 44th Amendment safeguard).

**Q2 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements regarding the appointment of the Chief Election Commissioner — which is/are correct?
*Answer approach:* Appointed by the President; historically on the advice of the Council of Ministers, subject to evolving legal/legislative developments (track the latest applicable law/judgment on this appointment process, as it has been actively litigated/legislated in recent years).

**Q3 (Prelims).** With reference to the Union and State Legislatures, what is a "Money Bill" and how does it differ from a "Financial Bill"?
*Answer approach:* A Money Bill deals exclusively with matters listed in Article 110 (taxation, borrowing, Consolidated Fund) and can only originate in the Lok Sabha, with the Rajya Sabha's role limited to recommendations within 14 days; a Financial Bill may contain other provisions beyond Article 110 matters and follows the ordinary bill process (except for certain financial-specific procedural aspects).

**Q4 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the National Human Rights Commission (NHRC): is it a constitutional, statutory, or executive body?
*Answer approach:* NHRC is a **statutory body**, constituted under the Protection of Human Rights Act, 1993 — not a constitutional body (a common trap, since many candidates conflate "important" with "constitutional").

**Q5 (Prelims).** With reference to the Panchayati Raj system, consider the following statements about the 73rd Constitutional Amendment.
*Answer approach:* Established a three-tier Panchayati Raj structure, mandated Gram Sabha, reservation for SC/ST/women, State Election Commission for local body elections, State Finance Commission for devolution — added the Eleventh Schedule listing 29 subjects.

**Q6 (Prelims).** Which of the following bodies is/are classified as quasi-judicial?
*Answer approach:* Bodies like the Competition Commission of India (CCI), National Green Tribunal (NGT), and Central Information Commission (CIC) perform quasi-judicial functions (adjudicating disputes, imposing penalties) while being administrative/statutory bodies, not constitutional courts.

**Q7 (Prelims).** With reference to the Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSPs), which Article/Part are they contained in, and are they justiciable?
*Answer approach:* Part IV, Articles 36-51; explicitly stated (Article 37) as **not enforceable by any court**, but fundamental in the governance of the country — a recurring conceptual distinction tested against Fundamental Rights (Part III, justiciable).

**Q8 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the "basic structure doctrine" — in which case was it propounded?
*Answer approach:* Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala (1973) — held that Parliament's constituent power to amend the Constitution under Article 368 does not extend to altering its "basic structure" (an evolving, judicially-defined list including federalism, judicial review, secularism, rule of law, etc.).

**Q9 (Mains, GS2, 15 marks).** Discuss the evolution and significance of the "basic structure doctrine" in Indian constitutional law.
*Model answer approach:* Introduction — origin in Kesavananda Bharati (1973), context of the Golaknath-vs-Parliament tussle over fundamental rights amendability. Body — (a) key basic structure elements identified over subsequent cases (federalism, secularism, judicial review, free & fair elections, rule of law), (b) its role as a check on parliamentary majoritarianism, (c) criticism — perceived judicial overreach into the legislature's constituent power, (d) balance achieved — protects constitutional core while allowing legitimate amendment. Conclusion — the doctrine remains a cornerstone safeguard of India's constitutional democracy.

**Q10 (Mains, GS2, 10 marks).** Examine the role of the Finance Commission in Centre-State fiscal relations.
*Model answer approach:* Constitutional body (Article 280); recommends vertical (Centre-States) and horizontal (inter-state) devolution of tax revenues; recommends grants-in-aid; recent commissions' criteria (population, income distance, area, forest cover, demographic performance); tension points — states' concerns over reduced fiscal autonomy versus need for equalization.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. Which Article deals with the Right to Constitutional Remedies? (Article 32, called the "heart and soul" of the Constitution by Dr. Ambedkar)
12. The Anti-Defection Law is contained in which Schedule? (Tenth Schedule, added by the 52nd Amendment)
13. Which body recommends the distribution of net proceeds of taxes between the Centre and States? (Finance Commission)
14. The Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) submits reports to whom? (The President, who causes them to be laid before Parliament)
15. What is the minimum age to become a member of the Rajya Sabha? (30 years, versus 25 years for Lok Sabha)
16. Which List does "Police" fall under in the Seventh Schedule? (State List)
17. The "Zero Hour" in Parliament refers to what? (An informal period immediately after Question Hour for raising urgent matters without prior notice)
18. Which constitutional amendment reduced the voting age from 21 to 18? (61st Amendment, 1989)
19. What is the tenure of members of the Union Public Service Commission? (6 years or until 65 years of age, whichever is earlier)
20. Which Article empowers the President to promulgate Ordinances? (Article 123, when Parliament is not in session)

---

### B.4 Economy — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** With reference to Fiscal Deficit, consider the following statements — which is/are correct?
- Fiscal Deficit = Total Expenditure − Total Receipts (excluding borrowings).
- It represents the total borrowing requirement of the government in a given year.
- A rising fiscal deficit is not automatically bad — depends on whether borrowed funds are used for capital/productive expenditure or revenue/consumption expenditure.
- The FRBM Act, 2003 provides a statutory ceiling/target for fiscal deficit as a percentage of GDP.

**Q2 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the Reverse Repo Rate:
- It is the rate at which RBI borrows money from commercial banks.
- It is used to absorb excess liquidity from the banking system.
- A hike in Reverse Repo Rate makes it more attractive for banks to park funds with RBI, reducing money supply in the economy.
- It is one component of the RBI's Liquidity Adjustment Facility (LAF), alongside the Repo Rate.

**Q3 (Prelims).** With reference to Goods and Services Tax (GST), which of the following is/are true?
- It is a destination-based, multi-stage, value-added indirect tax.
- It subsumed multiple central and state indirect taxes (excise duty, service tax, VAT, etc.).
- It has multiple tax slabs, with certain items (e.g., alcohol, petroleum) currently kept outside GST.
- The GST Council, a constitutional body under Article 279A, decides rates and administrative issues.

**Q4 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements regarding Special Drawing Rights (SDR):
- SDR is an international reserve asset created by the IMF.
- Its value is based on a basket of major global currencies.
- It is not a currency itself but can be exchanged for freely usable currencies among IMF members.

**Q5 (Prelims).** With reference to Non-Performing Assets (NPAs) in the Indian banking sector:
- An asset becomes an NPA when interest/principal payment remains overdue for a specified period (typically 90 days).
- High NPAs reduce banks' capacity to lend, affecting credit growth in the economy.
- Mechanisms to address NPAs include the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (IBC), Asset Reconstruction Companies, and bank recapitalization.

**Q6 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the Monetary Policy Committee (MPC):
- It is a 6-member body responsible for fixing the benchmark policy interest rate (Repo Rate).
- It was constituted following an amendment to the RBI Act, 1934.
- The MPC's primary mandate is to maintain price stability while keeping growth in mind, with an inflation target range set by the Government in consultation with RBI.

**Q7 (Prelims).** With reference to the Balance of Payments, classify each of the following under Current Account or Capital Account:
- Merchandise exports/imports → Current Account.
- Foreign Direct Investment inflows → Capital Account.
- Remittances from Indians working abroad → Current Account.
- External Commercial Borrowings → Capital Account.

**Q8 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about Minimum Support Price (MSP):
- MSP is announced by the Government based on recommendations of the Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices (CACP).
- It is currently not backed by a statutory legal guarantee for all crops (a major recent policy debate).
- Procurement primarily benefits wheat and rice farmers in a few states more than farmers of other crops/regions.

**Q9 (Mains, GS3, 15 marks).** Discuss the challenges in the implementation of the Goods and Services Tax (GST) in India and suggest measures for improvement.
- Introduction: define GST's "one nation, one tax" objective.
- Challenge 1: Multiple tax slabs causing classification disputes and compliance complexity.
- Challenge 2: Technology/GSTN portal glitches affecting return filing, especially for small businesses.
- Challenge 3: Revenue shortfall for states and compensation cess disputes.
- Challenge 4: Exclusion of petroleum, alcohol from GST ambit, fragmenting the tax base.
- Way forward: slab rationalization, improved GSTN infrastructure, phased inclusion of excluded items, strengthened GST Council federalism.
- Conclusion: GST remains a landmark reform requiring continuous calibration, not a one-time fix.

**Q10 (Mains, GS3, 10 marks).** Examine the significance of Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) in improving welfare delivery in India.
- Reduces leakages and duplication by transferring benefits directly to Aadhaar-linked bank accounts.
- Improves targeting accuracy, reducing "ghost beneficiaries."
- Challenges: financial/digital exclusion of the most vulnerable, connectivity issues in remote areas, exclusion errors.
- Way forward: last-mile banking correspondent expansion, grievance redressal mechanisms.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. What does CRR (Cash Reserve Ratio) require banks to maintain? (A minimum percentage of deposits as cash reserve with RBI, not earning interest)
12. Which index measures wholesale-level price changes in India? (Wholesale Price Index, WPI)
13. What is "Stagflation"? (Simultaneous stagnant growth and high inflation)
14. Which body recommends the Minimum Support Price? (Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices, CACP)
15. What is "Twin Balance Sheet Problem"? (Simultaneous stress in bank balance sheets — NPAs — and corporate balance sheets — over-leveraged debt)
16. What is India's central bank's primary inflation target range (illustrative, check current mandate)? (Typically around 4% with a +/-2% tolerance band)
17. What does "Current Account Deficit" indicate? (Imports of goods, services, and transfers exceed exports)
18. Which sector is described as the "backbone of the Indian economy" employing the largest workforce share? (Agriculture, though contributing a smaller share of GDP than employment share)
19. What is "Disinvestment"? (Government selling its stake in Public Sector Undertakings)
20. What is the difference between Repo Rate and Bank Rate? (Repo Rate involves collateralized short-term borrowing under LAF; Bank Rate is a longer-term, typically uncollateralized rate, historically linked to penal rates)

### B.5 Environment & Ecology — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements regarding Biodiversity Hotspots:
- A region qualifies as a hotspot if it has at least 1,500 endemic vascular plant species and has lost at least 70% of its original habitat.
- India has four recognized global biodiversity hotspots: Himalaya, Indo-Burma, Western Ghats-Sri Lanka, and Sundaland.
- Hotspots represent areas of both high biodiversity value and high threat level simultaneously.

**Q2 (Prelims).** With reference to Ramsar Sites, which of the following statements is/are correct?
- Ramsar Sites are wetlands designated under the Ramsar Convention for their ecological significance.
- India has a growing number of Ramsar Sites spread across multiple states.
- Designation does not transfer land ownership; it is a conservation commitment by the member country.

**Q3 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the IUCN Red List categories:
- Categories include Extinct, Extinct in the Wild, Critically Endangered, Endangered, Vulnerable, Near Threatened, and Least Concern.
- A species' category can change over time based on updated population/threat assessments.
- The Red List is compiled by the International Union for Conservation of Nature, not a government body.

**Q4 (Prelims).** With reference to the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972, consider the following statements:
- It classifies species into different Schedules with varying degrees of protection.
- Schedule I offers the highest degree of protection with the most stringent penalties.
- It provides the legal framework for declaring National Parks, Wildlife Sanctuaries, and Tiger Reserves.

**Q5 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about the Paris Agreement (2015):
- It is a legally binding international treaty on climate change under the UNFCCC.
- Its central goal is to limit global temperature rise to well below 2°C, pursuing efforts to limit it to 1.5°C, above pre-industrial levels.
- Unlike the Kyoto Protocol's top-down approach, it follows a bottom-up approach via Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs).

**Q6 (Prelims).** With reference to greenhouse gases, which has the highest Global Warming Potential per molecule among common ones?
- Sulphur hexafluoride and certain fluorinated gases typically have very high GWP relative to CO2.
- Methane has a significantly higher GWP than CO2 over a 20-year period, though shorter atmospheric lifetime.
- CO2 is used as the baseline (GWP = 1) for comparison.

**Q7 (Prelims).** Consider the following pairs of international environmental conventions and their focus area:
- Basel Convention → Transboundary movement of hazardous wastes.
- Cartagena Protocol → Biosafety, particularly living modified organisms.
- Nagoya Protocol → Access and benefit-sharing of genetic resources.
- Montreal Protocol → Phasing out substances that deplete the ozone layer.

**Q8 (Prelims).** With reference to Project Tiger and Tiger Reserves, consider the following statements:
- Tiger Reserves have a core (critical tiger habitat) and buffer zone structure.
- The National Tiger Conservation Authority oversees the implementation of Project Tiger.
- India hosts a majority of the world's wild tiger population.

**Q9 (Mains, GS3, 15 marks).** Discuss India's commitments under the Paris Agreement and the challenges in achieving its Nationally Determined Contributions.
- Introduction: India's NDC framework includes emission intensity reduction, non-fossil fuel capacity targets, and carbon sink enhancement goals.
- Challenge 1: Balancing developmental needs/energy access with emission reduction commitments.
- Challenge 2: Financing gap for renewable energy transition and climate adaptation.
- Challenge 3: Technology transfer dependency from developed nations.
- Challenge 4: Coal dependency for energy security in the near-to-medium term.
- Way forward: accelerated renewable capacity addition, green hydrogen mission, international climate finance mobilization.
- Conclusion: India's climate action must balance equity (differentiated responsibility) with urgency.

**Q10 (Mains, GS3, 10 marks).** Examine the effectiveness of the Environmental Impact Assessment (EIA) process in India.
- Purpose: assess environmental consequences of a project before clearance is granted.
- Strengths: structured public consultation process, expert appraisal committees.
- Weaknesses: dilution concerns in draft notifications, post-facto clearance controversies, weak monitoring/compliance after clearance is granted.
- Way forward: strengthen independent monitoring, digitize and make the process more transparent, ensure genuine public consultation.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. Which is India's first Ramsar-designated wetland? (Chilika Lake, Odisha, along with Keoladeo National Park among the earliest designated)
12. What does "COP" stand for in the context of climate summits? (Conference of the Parties, under the UNFCCC)
13. Which gas layer protects Earth from harmful UV radiation? (Ozone layer, in the stratosphere)
14. What is "Eutrophication"? (Excessive nutrient enrichment of a water body, causing algal blooms and oxygen depletion)
15. Which is the apex body for environmental clearance appeals/disputes in India? (National Green Tribunal, NGT)
16. What is a "Biosphere Reserve"? (A large protected area with core, buffer, and transition zones, balancing conservation with sustainable use)
17. Which convention specifically deals with international trade in endangered species? (CITES — Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species)
18. What is "Carbon Sequestration"? (The process of capturing and storing atmospheric carbon dioxide, e.g., via forests or geological storage)
19. Which Indian city/region is frequently cited for severe air pollution due to stubble burning and vehicular emissions? (Delhi-NCR)
20. What is the "Green Climate Fund"? (A UNFCCC mechanism to assist developing countries in climate mitigation and adaptation, financed by developed nations)

### B.6 Science & Technology — Question Bank

**Q1 (Prelims).** With reference to India's space program, consider the following statements:
- ISRO's PSLV (Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle) is primarily used for launching satellites into polar/sun-synchronous orbits.
- GSLV (Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle) is used for heavier payloads into geostationary transfer orbit.
- Chandrayaan and Mangalyaan missions demonstrated India's low-cost interplanetary/lunar exploration capability.

**Q2 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about CRISPR-Cas9 technology:
- It is a gene-editing tool that allows precise modification of DNA sequences.
- It has applications in agriculture (crop improvement), medicine (genetic disease treatment), and research.
- Ethical concerns include potential misuse for human germline editing.

**Q3 (Prelims).** With reference to vaccine technology, classify the following platforms:
- mRNA vaccines → deliver genetic instructions for cells to produce a viral protein, triggering immune response.
- Vector vaccines → use a modified, harmless virus to deliver genetic material.
- Inactivated/killed vaccines → use a virus that has been inactivated, unable to cause disease but still triggering immunity.

**Q4 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about Blockchain Technology:
- It is a decentralized, distributed ledger technology recording transactions across multiple computers.
- It underlies cryptocurrencies but also has applications in supply chain management and land record digitization.
- Its key features include immutability and transparency of recorded transactions.

**Q5 (Prelims).** With reference to India's indigenous defence systems, match the following:
- BrahMos → supersonic cruise missile (India-Russia joint venture).
- Agni series → long-range ballistic missiles.
- Akash → surface-to-air missile system.
- Tejas → indigenous light combat aircraft.

**Q6 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about Artificial Intelligence in governance:
- AI applications in governance include predictive policing, welfare scheme targeting, and chatbot-based citizen services.
- Concerns include algorithmic bias, data privacy, and lack of explainability in decision-making.

**Q7 (Prelims).** With reference to Nanotechnology, consider the following statements:
- It involves manipulation of matter at the nanoscale (typically 1-100 nanometers).
- Applications span medicine (targeted drug delivery), electronics (smaller/faster chips), and materials science (stronger, lighter materials).

**Q8 (Prelims).** Consider the following statements about Intellectual Property Rights (IPR):
- Patents protect inventions; Copyrights protect original literary/artistic works; Trademarks protect brand identifiers; Geographical Indications (GI) protect region-specific products.
- India is a signatory to international IPR frameworks including TRIPS under the WTO.

**Q9 (Mains, GS3, 15 marks).** Discuss the applications and ethical concerns associated with gene-editing technologies.
- Introduction: define gene-editing (CRISPR-Cas9) and its precision advantage over earlier techniques.
- Applications: disease-resistant crops, treatment of genetic disorders, potential eradication of vector-borne diseases via gene drives.
- Ethical concerns: germline editing implications for future generations, "designer babies" debate, unequal access exacerbating inequality, ecological risks of gene drives in wild populations.
- Way forward: robust regulatory frameworks, international consensus on red lines (e.g., germline editing bans), equitable access policies.
- Conclusion: balance innovation with precaution and ethical oversight.

**Q10 (Mains, GS3, 10 marks).** Examine the role of Artificial Intelligence in transforming public service delivery in India.
- Applications: chatbots for citizen grievance redressal, predictive analytics for welfare targeting, fraud detection in subsidy disbursal.
- Benefits: efficiency gains, reduced human discretion/corruption in routine decisions, scalability.
- Challenges: algorithmic bias, data privacy concerns, digital divide excluding non-tech-savvy citizens, lack of accountability/explainability.
- Way forward: a national AI governance framework, capacity building, human-in-the-loop safeguards for high-stakes decisions.

**Q11-Q20 (rapid-fire Prelims-style, self-test):**
11. Which is India's reusable launch vehicle technology demonstrator program? (RLV-TD, related to future reusable launch vehicle development)
12. What is "Gene Therapy"? (Treating disease by introducing, removing, or altering genetic material within a patient's cells)
13. What does "5G" primarily improve over "4G"? (Higher data speeds, lower latency, greater device connectivity density)
14. What is a "Quantum Computer" fundamentally based on? (Qubits, which can represent multiple states simultaneously via superposition, unlike classical bits)
15. Which Indian mission targeted the Sun for solar studies? (Aditya-L1)
16. What is "Stem Cell Therapy" used for? (Regenerating or repairing damaged tissues/organs using undifferentiated cells capable of developing into various cell types)
17. What is the primary purpose of the GAGAN system in India? (A satellite-based augmentation system improving GPS accuracy for aviation and other applications)
18. What is "Bt Cotton"? (A genetically modified cotton variety expressing a bacterial gene toxic to certain pests, e.g., bollworm)
19. What does "IoT" stand for and mean? (Internet of Things — network of interconnected physical devices exchanging data)
20. Which Indian institute/organization is primarily responsible for nuclear research? (Bhabha Atomic Research Centre, BARC)

### B.7 Current Affairs — Question Bank (Illustrative Format — Update Continuously With Latest Year's Events)

**Q1.** A newly launched government scheme aimed at X — which ministry administers it, what is its objective, and what is its target beneficiary group?
- Approach: build this as a standing template — for every new scheme in the news, fill in Ministry / Objective / Target Group / Key Feature / Launch Year in your running Schemes Master Table (Section 11.4).

**Q2.** A recently released report/index (e.g., Global Hunger Index, Human Development Index, Ease of Doing Business) — which organization publishes it, and what is India's recent ranking trend?
- Approach: maintain a running Reports & Indices table — Publisher / Frequency / Key Parameters / India's Recent Rank/Score/Trend.

**Q3.** A place recently in the news (due to a conflict, summit, natural disaster, or archaeological discovery) — where is it located, and why is it significant?
- Approach: maintain a running "Places in News" table — Location (state/country) / Reason for News / Relevant Static Linkage (geography/history/polity).

**Q4.** A recent Supreme Court/High Court judgment — what was the case about, and what constitutional principle did it invoke or clarify?
- Approach: tag every significant judgment against the relevant Polity concept (e.g., federalism, fundamental rights, judicial review) in your notes.

**Q5.** A recent international summit/agreement (G20, SCO, BRICS, COP, bilateral visits) — what were the key outcomes relevant to India?
- Approach: maintain a running IR events table — Summit/Visit / Key Outcomes / India's Stated Position / Relevant Bilateral or Multilateral Context.

**Q6-Q20:** Because current affairs content is inherently time-bound and this guide cannot predict future years' specific events, the correct preparation approach is to **apply the same five question-templates above to every current event you encounter daily**, converting passive newspaper reading into an active, exam-ready note-making habit (see Section 14.3-14.4 for the full method).

### B.8 Ethics (GS4) — Case Study & Conceptual Question Bank

**Q1 (Conceptual, 10 marks).** Distinguish between "Ethics" and "Law." Can an action be legal but unethical, or ethical but illegal? Give examples.
- Law is externally enforced by the state with formal sanctions; Ethics is an internalized sense of right/wrong, not always codified.
- Example of legal-but-unethical: exploiting a legal loophole to avoid tax obligations while technically compliant.
- Example of ethical-but-illegal (historically, in unjust legal regimes): civil disobedience against unjust laws (e.g., Gandhi's Salt Satyagraha, technically illegal under colonial law but widely regarded as ethically justified).

**Q2 (Conceptual, 10 marks).** What is "Emotional Intelligence," and why is it relevant for a civil servant?
- Defined by self-awareness, self-regulation, motivation, empathy, and social skill (Goleman's framework).
- Relevance: civil servants must manage stress, build trust across diverse stakeholders, and make empathetic yet fair decisions under pressure.

**Q3 (Conceptual, 10 marks).** Explain the concept of "Probity in Governance."
- Refers to scrupulous honesty, integrity, and adherence to ethical principles in public administration.
- Includes transparency, accountability, and freedom from corruption in the use of public office/funds.

**Q4 (Case Study, 15-20 marks).** You are a District Magistrate. During a natural disaster, you discover that relief funds are being diverted by a locally influential elected representative. Reporting this may cause political backlash and delay urgent relief work; not reporting compromises your integrity.
- Stakeholders: disaster-affected citizens, the elected representative, your superior officers, the broader public trust in governance.
- Ethical dilemmas: personal integrity vs. political/administrative pressure; short-term relief efficiency vs. long-term systemic accountability.
- Possible courses of action: (a) immediately report through proper channels while ensuring relief work continues in parallel via alternate arrangements, (b) confront the representative directly first, risking delay, (c) ignore the issue to avoid conflict — clearly the least ethical option.
- Recommended decision: pursue (a) — report through proper administrative/vigilance channels while safeguarding uninterrupted relief operations, upholding both probity and public welfare simultaneously.

**Q5 (Case Study, 15-20 marks).** As a senior officer, you are asked by a political superior to favor a particular contractor in a public tender, against your assessment of merit. Discuss the ethical dilemma and your course of action.
- Stakeholders: taxpayers, the contracting process's integrity, competing contractors, your career/institutional relationships.
- Ethical dilemmas: institutional loyalty/hierarchy vs. objectivity and non-partisanship (a core Civil Service value per GS4 syllabus).
- Possible courses of action: (a) politely decline citing merit-based evaluation norms and document the interaction, (b) comply to avoid conflict, compromising integrity, (c) escalate through proper administrative channels if pressure persists.
- Recommended decision: (a), escalating to (c) if pressure continues — objectivity and adherence to rules take precedence over political convenience.

**Q6-Q10 (Rapid conceptual self-test prompts):**
6. What did Kautilya's Arthashastra emphasize regarding a ruler's duty? (Welfare of subjects as the ruler's paramount duty — "in the happiness of his subjects lies the king's happiness")
7. What is "Whistleblowing," and what ethical tension does it involve? (Reporting wrongdoing within an organization; tension between loyalty to the organization and duty to the larger public interest)
8. What does "Conflict of Interest" mean in public administration? (A situation where an official's personal interests could improperly influence their official duties)
9. What is "Corporate Governance," and why is it discussed in the Ethics syllabus? (Systems of rules/practices directing and controlling a company; relevant because ethical governance principles apply across public and private institutional contexts)
10. What is the significance of Gandhi's talisman ("recall the face of the poorest and weakest man...") in public administration ethics? (A test for policy/decision-making — will this action help or harm the most vulnerable person you can recall?)

## 31. Appendix C: Answer Writing Templates & Model Answers

### C.1 Generic GS Answer Skeleton (10 Marks / ~100-120 words)

```
[Intro: 1-2 lines defining/contextualizing the keyword]
[Body point 1: dimension A, 1-2 lines]
[Body point 2: dimension B, 1-2 lines]
[Body point 3: dimension C, 1-2 lines]
[Conclusion: 1-2 lines, forward-looking]
```

### C.2 Generic GS Answer Skeleton (15 Marks / ~150-200 words)

```
[Intro: 2-3 lines with a fact/data point/definition]
[Body dimension 1 with sub-points, 3-4 lines]
[Body dimension 2 with sub-points, 3-4 lines]
[Body dimension 3 with sub-points, 3-4 lines]
[Body dimension 4 (optional) with sub-points, 2-3 lines]
[Conclusion: 2-3 lines, solution-oriented]
```

### C.3 Full Model Answer — GS2 (15 marks): "Discuss the significance of the Right to Information (RTI) Act in strengthening democratic accountability in India."

- Introduction: The RTI Act, 2005 operationalizes the constitutional right to freedom of speech and expression (Article 19(1)(a)) by enabling citizens to access information held by public authorities.
- Dimension 1 — Transparency: Citizens can scrutinize government decisions, expenditure, and policy implementation, reducing information asymmetry between the state and citizens.
- Dimension 2 — Accountability: RTI has exposed several instances of corruption and maladministration, strengthening the accountability chain beyond periodic elections alone.
- Dimension 3 — Empowerment: Marginalized citizens have used RTI to access entitlements (ration cards, pensions, welfare scheme status), making governance more citizen-centric.
- Dimension 4 — Challenges: Pendency of appeals before Information Commissions, vacancies in commissioner posts, and the "Public Interest Immunity" exemptions sometimes used to deny legitimate requests.
- Conclusion: Strengthening RTI's institutional machinery (timely appointments, digitization of requests, proactive disclosure under Section 4) is essential to fully realize its democratic potential.

### C.4 Full Model Answer — GS3 (15 marks): "Examine the challenges in achieving food security in India despite being a major agricultural producer."

- Introduction: Despite being among the world's largest producers of rice, wheat, and milk, India continues to face food security challenges reflected in indices like the Global Hunger Index.
- Dimension 1 — Production-side issues: Regional disparity in agricultural productivity, over-dependence on a few crops (rice-wheat), soil degradation from unsustainable practices.
- Dimension 2 — Distribution-side issues: Storage and supply chain inefficiencies, leakages in the Public Distribution System, post-harvest losses due to inadequate cold-chain infrastructure.
- Dimension 3 — Access-side issues: Poverty and income inequality limiting purchasing power despite physical availability of food; malnutrition persisting even in food-surplus regions.
- Dimension 4 — Policy response: National Food Security Act coverage, One Nation One Ration Card portability, e-technology in PDS (Aadhaar-linked distribution).
- Conclusion: Achieving genuine food security requires simultaneously addressing production diversification, distribution efficiency, and income-based access — not merely production growth alone.

### C.5 Full Model Answer — GS1 (10 marks): "Discuss the role of women in the Indian freedom struggle."

- Introduction: Women's participation transformed the freedom struggle from an elite-led movement into a genuinely mass movement.
- Point 1: Leaders like Sarojini Naidu, Kasturba Gandhi, and Aruna Asaf Ali provided political leadership and organizational mobilization.
- Point 2: Mass participation in the Salt Satyagraha and Quit India Movement demonstrated grassroots mobilization beyond urban elites.
- Point 3: Revolutionary women like Bhikaiji Cama and Pritilata Waddedar contributed to the armed-resistance strand of the movement.
- Conclusion: This participation also laid the groundwork for post-independence women's political and social rights movements.

### C.6 Full Model Answer — GS4 Case Study (20 marks): "You are an officer posted in a flood-hit district. Relief material is limited, and you must decide priority among multiple affected villages. Discuss your approach."

- Stakeholders: affected villagers across multiple locations, relief agencies, local political representatives, your administrative superiors.
- Ethical dilemma: equitable distribution vs. utilitarian triage (helping the most severely affected first) vs. political pressure to prioritize certain areas.
- Course of action 1: Conduct rapid, objective needs-assessment surveys across all villages before allocation — data-driven prioritization.
- Course of action 2: Prioritize based on severity of impact (loss of life risk, displacement numbers, availability of alternate resources) rather than political considerations.
- Course of action 3: Maintain complete transparency in the allocation criteria to preempt allegations of favoritism.
- Final decision: adopt a severity-and-need-based transparent allocation framework (Course 1+2+3 combined), documenting the rationale to ensure both fairness and accountability.

### C.7 Full Model Essay Outline: "Technology as a double-edged sword: transforming society while widening inequalities"

- Introduction: Open with a contrast — technology's promise of universal empowerment against the reality of a persistent digital divide.
- Body 1 (Historical/Philosophical angle): Every major technological revolution (industrial, information) has historically produced both progress and disruption.
- Body 2 (Contemporary/Economic angle): Digital technology has enabled financial inclusion (UPI, DBT) while automation threatens low-skill employment.
- Body 3 (Social angle): EdTech and telemedicine have expanded access in remote areas, yet the digital divide (device/connectivity access) excludes the poorest.
- Body 4 (Way forward/Global comparison): Policy measures — digital literacy missions, affordable broadband expansion, algorithmic accountability regulation.
- Conclusion: Technology's net impact on equality is not predetermined by the technology itself, but by the deliberateness of the policy choices surrounding its deployment.

| 12 | Right to Education Act | 2009 | Free and compulsory education, ages 6-14 |
| 13 | Land Acquisition Act (new) | 2013 | Fair compensation and resettlement framework |
| 14 | Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code | 2016 | Time-bound insolvency resolution framework |
| 15 | Goods and Services Tax Acts | 2017 | Unified indirect tax regime |
| 16 | Personal Data Protection/DPDP Act | 2023 | Digital personal data protection framework |
| 17 | Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (replacing IPC) | 2023 | Overhaul of criminal law framework |

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which Article deals with the Uniform Civil Code (as a DPSP)? | Article 44 |
| 2 | Who is called the "Father of the Indian Constitution"? | Dr. B.R. Ambedkar |
| 3 | Which schedule lists the languages recognized under the Constitution? | Eighth Schedule |
| 4 | What is the minimum age for the office of the President of India? | 35 years |
| 5 | Which body conducts the Civil Services Examination? | Union Public Service Commission |
| 6 | Who appoints the Governor of a State? | The President of India |
| 7 | What is the term of office of the Lok Sabha (unless dissolved earlier)? | 5 years |
| 8 | Which House can move a No-Confidence Motion? | Lok Sabha only |
| 9 | What is the full form of NITI Aayog? | National Institution for Transforming India |
| 10 | Who is the ex-officio Chairman of NITI Aayog? | The Prime Minister |
| 11 | Which Constitutional Amendment added Fundamental Duties? | 42nd Amendment (1976) |
| 12 | How many Fundamental Duties are currently listed? | 11 |
| 13 | Which body regulates the securities market in India? | Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) |
| 14 | Which organization regulates insurance in India? | Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority (IRDAI) |
| 15 | What is India's central bank called? | Reserve Bank of India (RBI) |
| 16 | In which year was RBI established? | 1935 |
| 17 | Which index tracks retail/consumer-level inflation in India? | Consumer Price Index (CPI) |
| 18 | Which ministry releases the Economic Survey? | Ministry of Finance |
| 19 | Who presents the Economic Survey in Parliament? | The Chief Economic Adviser (tabled by the Finance Minister) |
| 20 | What is India's currency regulator/issuer? | Reserve Bank of India |
| 21 | Which Five-Year Plan focused on the Mahalanobis model? | 2nd Five-Year Plan |
| 22 | What is the full form of GST? | Goods and Services Tax |
| 23 | Who chairs the GST Council? | The Union Finance Minister |
| 24 | Which Article establishes the GST Council? | Article 279A |
| 25 | What is India's apex agricultural credit institution? | NABARD (National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development) |
| 26 | Which mission aims for "Housing for All"? | Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana |
| 27 | What is the full form of MGNREGA? | Mahatma Gandhi National Rural Employment Guarantee Act |
| 28 | How many days of guaranteed employment does MGNREGA provide per household per year? | 100 days |
| 29 | Which body prepares the Human Development Index? | United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) |
| 30 | Which body prepares the Global Hunger Index? | Concern Worldwide and Welthungerhilfe |
| 31 | What does IPCC stand for? | Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change |
| 32 | Which protocol specifically targets ozone-depleting substances? | Montreal Protocol |
| 33 | Which convention deals with wetlands of international importance? | Ramsar Convention |
| 34 | What is India's first national park (chronologically)? | Jim Corbett National Park (established 1936, originally Hailey National Park) |
| 35 | Which is the largest biosphere reserve in India (illustrative)? | Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve (one of the largest and first declared) |
| 36 | Which gas is primarily responsible for the greenhouse effect in largest volume terms? | Carbon dioxide |
| 37 | What is the chemical formula for common ozone-depleting CFCs' category? | Chlorofluorocarbons |
| 38 | Which Indian mission targeted Mars? | Mangalyaan (Mars Orbiter Mission) |
| 39 | In which year did India launch Chandrayaan-1? | 2008 |
| 40 | What is ISRO's heaviest operational launch vehicle series (illustrative)? | LVM3 (formerly GSLV Mk III) |
| 41 | Who administers India's satellite navigation system (NavIC)? | ISRO |
| 42 | What is the full form of DRDO? | Defence Research and Development Organisation |
| 43 | Which missile system is India's indigenous supersonic cruise missile (joint venture)? | BrahMos |
| 44 | What is India's indigenous aircraft carrier called? | INS Vikrant |
| 45 | Which force guards India's international borders with Pakistan primarily? | Border Security Force (BSF) |
| 46 | Which force guards the India-China border primarily? | Indo-Tibetan Border Police (ITBP) |
| 47 | What is the full form of NIA? | National Investigation Agency |
| 48 | Which body probes economic offences and money laundering in India? | Enforcement Directorate (ED) |
| 49 | What is the full form of CBI? | Central Bureau of Investigation |
| 50 | Which constitutional article deals with the establishment of the CAG? | Article 148 |
| 51 | What is the tenure of the CAG? | 6 years or 65 years of age, whichever is earlier |
| 52 | Which body appoints judges to the Supreme Court (post-collegium system evolution)? | The Collegium system (Chief Justice + senior judges), formal appointment by the President |
| 53 | What is "Public Interest Litigation" (PIL)? | A legal mechanism allowing any citizen/group to seek judicial remedy for public interest issues, not just directly affected parties |
| 54 | Which case established the "basic structure doctrine"? | Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala (1973) |
| 55 | What is "Judicial Review"? | The power of courts to examine the constitutionality of legislative/executive actions |
| 56 | Which body regulates telecom services in India? | Telecom Regulatory Authority of India (TRAI) |
| 57 | Which body regulates competition/anti-trust matters in India? | Competition Commission of India (CCI) |
| 58 | What does FDI stand for? | Foreign Direct Investment |
| 59 | What does FPI stand for? | Foreign Portfolio Investment |
| 60 | What is the difference between FDI and FPI? | FDI involves control/long-term stake in an enterprise; FPI is passive investment in financial assets without control |
| 61 | Which body compiles India's national income accounts (GDP data)? | National Statistical Office (NSO), Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation |
| 62 | What is the base year commonly used for India's current GDP series (illustrative, check latest)? | 2011-12 (subject to periodic revision) |
| 63 | What is "Green GDP"? | A GDP measure adjusted for environmental degradation/depletion of natural resources |
| 64 | What is the "Demographic Dividend"? | Economic growth potential from a large working-age population relative to dependents |
| 65 | What is India's current broad population trend relevant to demographic dividend discussions? | A relatively young population structure compared to many developed/aging economies |
| 66 | Which sector employs the largest share of India's workforce? | Agriculture sector |
| 67 | Which sector contributes the largest share to India's GDP? | Services sector |
| 68 | What is "Disguised Unemployment"? | A situation where more people are employed in an activity than actually needed, with marginal productivity near zero |
| 69 | What is "Structural Unemployment"? | Unemployment caused by a mismatch between workers' skills and available job requirements |
| 70 | What is "Universal Basic Income" (UBI)? | A policy proposal providing all citizens a regular, unconditional cash payment |
| 71 | Which body releases the Ease of Doing Business rankings historically? | World Bank (discontinued the report in its earlier form; check current status) |
| 72 | What is "Sovereign Gold Bond"? | A government security denominated in grams of gold, an alternative to holding physical gold |
| 73 | What is a "Masala Bond"? | Rupee-denominated bonds issued outside India to raise funds from international markets |
| 74 | Which institution issues Masala Bonds' regulatory framework? | Reserve Bank of India, in coordination with SEBI |
| 75 | What is the difference between "Revenue Expenditure" and "Capital Expenditure"? | Revenue expenditure does not create assets/reduce liabilities (e.g., salaries); Capital expenditure creates assets or reduces liabilities (e.g., infrastructure investment) |
| 76 | What is "Zero-Based Budgeting"? | A budgeting method where every expense must be justified from scratch each period, not based on the previous period's baseline |
| 77 | What is "Deficit Financing"? | Financing a fiscal deficit through borrowing or, historically, monetary expansion |
| 78 | What does "Base Erosion and Profit Shifting" (BEPS) refer to? | Tax avoidance strategies exploiting gaps between different countries' tax rules |
| 79 | What is the "Laffer Curve"? | A theoretical relationship showing tax revenue can decline beyond an optimal tax rate due to reduced economic activity |
| 80 | What is "Quantitative Easing"? | A central bank's large-scale asset purchase program to inject liquidity into the economy |
| 81 | Who is regarded as the architect of India's 1991 economic reforms as Finance Minister? | Dr. Manmohan Singh |
| 82 | Under whose Prime Ministership were the 1991 reforms initiated? | P.V. Narasimha Rao |
| 83 | What is "Balance of Payments" (BoP)? | A systematic record of all economic transactions between a country's residents and the rest of the world |
| 84 | What triggers a "Currency Devaluation"? | A deliberate downward adjustment of a country's currency value relative to another currency/standard, under a fixed/managed exchange rate regime |
| 85 | What is "Special Economic Zone" (SEZ)? | A designated area with distinct economic regulations to promote exports/investment |
| 86 | What is the full form of DPIIT? | Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade |
| 87 | Which Ministry oversees the Start-Up India initiative? | Ministry of Commerce and Industry (via DPIIT) |
| 88 | What is "Angel Tax" (in Indian startup context)? | Tax levied on excess share premium received by unlisted companies, historically a point of startup-sector concern |
| 89 | What is a "Unicorn" startup? | A privately held startup valued at over USD 1 billion |
| 90 | What does "MSME" stand for? | Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises |
| 91 | Which Act/framework currently classifies MSMEs in India (by investment and turnover)? | The MSME classification framework under the Ministry of MSME (revised criteria, check latest thresholds) |
| 92 | What is the "Genome India Project"? | A national initiative to map the genetic diversity of India's population |
| 93 | What is "mRNA vaccine technology" primarily used for (in recent prominent examples)? | Delivering genetic instructions to cells to produce an antigen, triggering an immune response (e.g., certain COVID-19 vaccines) |
| 94 | What is "Antimicrobial Resistance" (AMR)? | The ability of microorganisms to resist the effects of drugs that once could successfully treat them |
| 95 | What is "One Health" approach? | An integrated approach recognizing the interconnection between human, animal, and environmental health |
| 96 | What is "Precision Agriculture"? | Use of technology (sensors, GPS, data analytics) to optimize farming input use and yield |
| 97 | What is "Vertical Farming"? | Growing crops in vertically stacked layers, often in controlled indoor environments |
| 98 | What is "Blue Economy"? | Sustainable use of ocean resources for economic growth while preserving marine ecosystem health |
| 99 | What is "Circular Economy"? | An economic model emphasizing reuse, recycling, and minimizing waste, as opposed to a linear "take-make-dispose" model |
| 100 | What is "Just Transition" (in climate policy context)? | Ensuring a fair shift away from fossil-fuel-dependent livelihoods towards a green economy, protecting affected workers/communities |

### D.16 Rapid-Fire Mega Q&A Bank — Part 2 (Prelims Self-Test Format)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which Article of the Constitution deals with Emergency due to war/external aggression? | Article 352 (National Emergency) |
| 2 | Which Article deals with President's Rule in a state? | Article 356 |
| 3 | Which Article deals with Financial Emergency? | Article 360 |
| 4 | Which body recommends State Finance Commission's devolution to local bodies? | State Finance Commission itself, constituted under Article 243-I/243-Y |
| 5 | What is the Eleventh Schedule related to? | Subjects assigned to Panchayats (73rd Amendment) |
| 6 | What is the Twelfth Schedule related to? | Subjects assigned to Municipalities (74th Amendment) |
| 7 | Which body appoints the Chief Justice of India? | The President, based on convention/collegium recommendation |
| 8 | What is "Original Jurisdiction" of the Supreme Court? | Cases the Supreme Court can hear directly, not on appeal, e.g., Centre-State disputes |
| 9 | What is "Writ Jurisdiction"? | Power of courts to issue writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto) for enforcing rights |
| 10 | What does "Habeas Corpus" mean? | "To have the body" — a writ to produce a detained person before the court |
| 11 | What does "Mandamus" mean? | "We command" — a writ directing a public authority to perform its duty |
| 12 | What does "Quo Warranto" mean? | "By what authority" — a writ questioning the legality of a person's claim to a public office |
| 13 | What is a "Hung Assembly/Parliament"? | A legislature where no single party/coalition has a clear majority |
| 14 | What is "Anti-Defection Law" primarily meant to prevent? | Elected members switching parties for personal gain, undermining electoral mandate |
| 15 | Who decides disqualification under the Anti-Defection Law? | The Presiding Officer (Speaker/Chairman) of the House, subject to judicial review |
| 16 | What is the "Doctrine of Basic Structure" primarily a check on? | Parliament's constituent power to amend the Constitution under Article 368 |
| 17 | Which body is responsible for delimitation of constituencies? | Delimitation Commission |
| 18 | What is "First Past The Post" (FPTP) electoral system? | A candidate winning with the most votes in a constituency wins, regardless of majority share |
| 19 | What is "Proportional Representation"? | Seats allocated in proportion to votes received by each party (used in Rajya Sabha elections, for example) |
| 20 | What is a "Money Bill" restricted to originate from? | The Lok Sabha only |
| 21 | What are "Ordinary Bills"? | Bills that can originate in either House of Parliament and require passage by both |
| 22 | What is the "Consolidated Fund of India"? | The primary government fund into which all revenues/loans are credited, requiring parliamentary approval for withdrawal |
| 23 | What is the "Contingency Fund of India"? | A fund for unforeseen expenditure, operated by the Executive, replenished later by Parliament |
| 24 | What is the "Public Account of India"? | Account holding funds like provident funds/small savings, not requiring the same appropriation process |
| 25 | What is a "Cut Motion" in Parliament? | A motion to reduce a demand for grants, used to express disapproval |
| 26 | What is a "Calling Attention Motion"? | A procedural device for a member to call a minister's attention to an urgent public matter |
| 27 | What is the "Question Hour"? | The first hour of a parliamentary sitting, dedicated to questions to ministers |
| 28 | What is the "Zero Hour"? | An informal period after Question Hour for raising urgent matters without prior notice |
| 29 | What is a "Whip" in parliamentary context? | A party functionary ensuring members vote according to the party line |
| 30 | What is "Sine Die" adjournment? | Adjournment of a House without setting a date for reassembly |
| 31 | What does "Prorogation" mean? | The formal end of a parliamentary session by the President |
| 32 | What is "Dissolution" of the Lok Sabha? | The end of the House's term, requiring fresh general elections |
| 33 | What is the "Rule of Law"? | The principle that all persons, including the government, are subject to and accountable under the law |
| 34 | What is "Judicial Activism"? | Courts taking a proactive role in policy matters, sometimes going beyond traditional adjudication |
| 35 | What is a "PIL" primarily used for? | Enabling public-spirited individuals/groups to seek judicial remedy for broader public interest issues |
| 36 | What is "Collective Responsibility"? | The principle that the Council of Ministers is collectively answerable to the Lok Sabha |
| 37 | What is "Individual Responsibility" of a minister? | A minister's personal accountability to the President for their ministry's conduct |
| 38 | What is the "Council of Ministers" size cap linked to? | The 91st Amendment, capping total size at 15% of the total strength of the House |
| 39 | What is a "Coalition Government"? | A government formed by an alliance of multiple political parties |
| 40 | What is the "Anti-Defection" penalty for a defecting MP/MLA? | Disqualification from membership of the House |
| 41 | What is "Cooperative Federalism" best exemplified by (institutional example)? | The GST Council, involving joint Centre-State decision-making |
| 42 | What is "Fiscal Federalism"? | The division of financial powers and responsibilities between different levels of government |
| 43 | What is the "Inter-State Council"? | A constitutional body (Article 263) to facilitate coordination between states and the Centre |
| 44 | What is "Zonal Council"? | Statutory (not constitutional) bodies facilitating regional inter-state cooperation |
| 45 | What is a "Money Laundering" offence primarily concerned with? | Concealing the origins of illegally obtained money by passing it through legitimate channels |
| 46 | What is the "Prevention of Money Laundering Act" enforced by? | The Enforcement Directorate |
| 47 | What is "Left-Wing Extremism" (LWE) commonly linked to in India? | Naxalism, concentrated historically in parts of central-eastern India ("Red Corridor") |
| 48 | What is "Cyber Security" primarily concerned with in governance discussions? | Protecting digital infrastructure/data from unauthorized access, disruption, or damage |
| 49 | What is the "National Cyber Security Policy" aimed at? | Building a secure cyberspace ecosystem for citizens, businesses, and government |
| 50 | What does "Data Localization" mean? | Requiring data about a country's citizens to be stored within that country's borders |

### 33.1 Glossary of UPSC & Governance Terms

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| AIS | All India Services (IAS, IPS, IFoS) |
| DAF | Detailed Application Form, filled before the Interview stage |
| CSAT | Civil Services Aptitude Test (Prelims Paper II, qualifying) |
| GS | General Studies |
| PYQ | Previous Year Question(s) |
| NCERT | National Council of Educational Research and Training |
| DPSP | Directive Principles of State Policy |
| FR | Fundamental Rights |
| FD | Fundamental Duties |
| CAG | Comptroller and Auditor General of India |
| UPSC | Union Public Service Commission |
| EC | Election Commission (of India) |
| NHRC | National Human Rights Commission |
| NGT | National Green Tribunal |
| CCI | Competition Commission of India |
| RTI | Right to Information |
| RTE | Right to Education |
| MGNREGA | Mahatma Gandhi National Rural Employment Guarantee Act |
| PDS | Public Distribution System |
| MSP | Minimum Support Price |
| FRBM | Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (Act) |
| GST | Goods and Services Tax |
| RBI | Reserve Bank of India |
| MPC | Monetary Policy Committee |
| CRR | Cash Reserve Ratio |
| SLR | Statutory Liquidity Ratio |
| NPA | Non-Performing Asset |
| IBC | Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code |
| FDI | Foreign Direct Investment |
| FPI | Foreign Portfolio Investment |
| BoP | Balance of Payments |
| WPI | Wholesale Price Index |
| CPI | Consumer Price Index |
| GDP | Gross Domestic Product |
| GNP | Gross National Product |
| NITI Aayog | National Institution for Transforming India |
| EIA | Environmental Impact Assessment |
| IUCN | International Union for Conservation of Nature |
| CITES | Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species |
| UNFCCC | United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change |
| NDC | Nationally Determined Contribution (under Paris Agreement) |
| COP | Conference of the Parties (climate summit) |
| ISRO | Indian Space Research Organisation |
| DRDO | Defence Research and Development Organisation |
| AI | Artificial Intelligence |
| IoT | Internet of Things |
| IPR | Intellectual Property Rights |
| GI | Geographical Indication |
| WTO | World Trade Organization |
| IMF | International Monetary Fund |
| SDR | Special Drawing Rights |
| SEZ | Special Economic Zone |
| MSME | Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises |
| PLI | Production Linked Incentive |
| DBT | Direct Benefit Transfer |
| PIB | Press Information Bureau |
| PRS | PRS Legislative Research (parliamentary tracking body) |
| Mains | The written stage of UPSC CSE (Stage 2) |
| Prelims | The objective screening stage of UPSC CSE (Stage 1) |
| Optional Subject | Candidate-chosen subject for Mains Papers VI & VII |
| Qualifying Paper | A paper needing minimum marks but not counted in final merit |
| Merit-Ranking Paper | A paper whose marks count towards final rank |
| Cadre | The state/region a selected candidate is allocated to serve in |
| DM | District Magistrate |
| SP | Superintendent of Police |
| IFS | Indian Foreign Service (note: also sometimes used for Indian Forest Service — check context) |
| IRS | Indian Revenue Service |
| IAS | Indian Administrative Service |
| IPS | Indian Police Service |
| IFoS | Indian Forest Service |
| Ethics Case Study | Scenario-based question in GS4 testing applied ethical decision-making |
| Directive Word | The instruction verb in a Mains question (discuss/examine/critically analyze etc.) |
| Static Portion | Syllabus content that doesn't change year to year (history, polity basics, etc.) |
| Current Affairs | Recent events/developments tested alongside or fused with static content |
| Test Series | A structured series of mock exams with evaluation, used for practice |
| Sectional Test | A mock test focused on one subject/topic area only |
| Full-Length Mock | A complete mock test replicating the actual exam's full format and duration |
| DAF Dossier | A candidate's self-prepared reference document covering every DAF entry in depth |
| Board (Interview) | The panel of UPSC members conducting the Personality Test |
| Personality Test | The official term for the UPSC Interview stage |
| Attempt | Counted upon appearing in even one Prelims paper, regardless of clearing it |
| Category (UR/OBC/SC/ST/EWS) | Reservation classification affecting age limit, attempts, and cutoffs |
| PwBD | Persons with Benchmark Disabilities |
| NCL | Non-Creamy Layer (within OBC classification) |
| Cut-off | The minimum qualifying score/rank for a given stage/category |
| Negative Marking | Marks deducted for wrong answers in objective papers |
| Elimination Technique | A CSAT/Prelims strategy of ruling out clearly wrong options before answering |
| Data Sufficiency | A question format testing whether given data is enough to answer, without solving fully |
| RC | Reading Comprehension |
| DI | Data Interpretation |
| TSD | Time, Speed and Distance |
| SI/CI | Simple Interest / Compound Interest |
| Answer Writing | The skill of converting knowledge into structured, scored written responses |
| Model Answer | A high-quality reference answer used for self-evaluation/benchmarking |
| Skeleton/Template | A pre-planned structural outline for answering a category of questions |
| Word Limit | The recommended/enforced length for a given Mains answer |
| Static-Current Fusion | The technique of linking a current event to its underlying static concept |
| Master Table | A candidate's self-made consolidated reference table on a recurring topic type |
| Revision Cycle | One complete pass through the syllabus during preparation, repeated multiple times |
| Forgetting Curve | The natural decline of memory retention over time without revision |
| Spaced Repetition | A learning technique revisiting material at increasing intervals to combat forgetting |
| NCERT Foundation | The base-level conceptual literacy built using Class 6-12 NCERT textbooks |
| Standard Reference Book | A widely recommended, comprehensive book for a given UPSC subject |
| Compilation Notes | Condensed, exam-oriented notes (often coaching-institute-produced) on a topic |
| Schemes Master Table | A candidate's running reference of government schemes and their details |
| Bodies Master Table | A candidate's running reference of constitutional/statutory/regulatory bodies |
| Places in News | A running current-affairs table tracking geographically significant recent events |
| Reports & Indices | A running current-affairs table tracking recently released reports/rankings |
| Essay Skeleton | A pre-planned multidimensional structure for tackling an Essay topic |
| Ethics Talisman | Gandhi's guiding test recalling the poorest/weakest person when making decisions |
| Probity | Scrupulous honesty and integrity, especially in public office |
| Emotional Intelligence | The capacity to understand and manage one's own and others' emotions effectively |
| Basic Structure Doctrine | The judicially-evolved principle limiting Parliament's power to alter core constitutional features |
| Federalism | Division of powers between a central government and constituent state governments |
| Judicial Review | Courts' power to examine the constitutionality of laws/executive actions |
| Separation of Powers | Division of governmental functions among legislature, executive, and judiciary |
| Devolution | Transfer of powers/resources from a central authority to lower levels of government |
| Panchayati Raj | The three-tier system of rural local self-governance in India |
| Cooperative Federalism | A model emphasizing collaborative Centre-State policymaking |
| Competitive Federalism | A model emphasizing states competing with each other for investment/development outcomes |

### 33.2 Closing Summary

This guide has walked through the entire architecture of the UPSC Civil Services Examination — from the very first NCERT page a beginner opens, through every static subject's syllabus and book list, through the connective tissue of current affairs, through the conversion skill of answer writing, through Essay and Optional subject strategy, through systematically mined PYQs, through week-by-week study calendars for every starting point, through test series discipline, through the Personality Test, and finally through the psychological reality of a multi-year, low-success-rate exam.

If there is one idea to carry forward above all others, it is this: **UPSC is a learnable, systematic process, not a lottery of talent.** Thousands of candidates with no special genius, no coaching, and no shortcuts have cracked it using exactly the method laid out across these sections — NCERT foundation, standard books read and revised in cycles, a disciplined daily current affairs habit, consistent answer-writing practice from the earliest months, honest test-series-driven self-assessment, and a sustainable, health-protecting daily routine sustained over 12-24+ months.

Whichever plan you choose from Section 22, whichever optional you choose from Section 18, and whichever attempt this is for you — the process does not change. Read less, revise more. Write from day one. Track your own gaps honestly. Protect your sleep and your consistency more than your total hour count. And treat every mock test, every failed attempt, and every difficult PYQ as **data to close a specific gap**, never as a verdict on your worth or your eventual outcome.

Good luck with your preparation — and more importantly, good luck sustaining the daily discipline that actually determines the outcome.

---

## 35. Appendix F: Extended Mains PYQ-Style Practice Bank

A subject/paper-wise bank of Mains-style practice questions (10-15 marks format, ~150-word answers unless noted). Use these for timed answer-writing drills.

### F.1 GS Paper I (History, Geography, Society) — 25 Qs

| # | Topic | Question |
|---|-------|----------|
| 1 | Modern History | Assess the contribution of moderates and extremists to the freedom struggle before 1919. |
| 2 | Modern History | Examine the socio-economic impact of the Permanent Settlement on Bengal's peasantry. |
| 3 | Modern History | "The Revolt of 1857 was more a sepoy mutiny than a war of independence." Critically examine. |
| 4 | World History | Discuss the causes and consequences of the Industrial Revolution on colonial economies. |
| 5 | World History | Analyze how the Cold War shaped the process of decolonization in Asia and Africa. |
| 6 | Art & Culture | Discuss the salient features of Indo-Islamic architecture with examples. |
| 7 | Post-Independence | Evaluate the linguistic reorganization of states and its impact on Indian federalism. |
| 8 | Post-Independence | Discuss the role of Sardar Patel in the integration of princely states. |
| 9 | Society | Examine the impact of urbanization on the joint family system in India. |
| 10 | Society | Discuss the socio-economic implications of an ageing population in India. |
| 11 | Society | Analyze the role of women's self-help groups in rural empowerment. |
| 12 | Society | Discuss the causes and consequences of regionalism in India. |
| 13 | Society | Examine the changing nature of the caste system in urban India. |
| 14 | Geography | Explain the mechanism of the Indian monsoon and factors affecting its variability. |
| 15 | Geography | Discuss the factors responsible for the location of the iron and steel industry in India. |
| 16 | Geography | Examine the causes and impacts of urban heat islands in Indian metropolitan cities. |
| 17 | Geography | Discuss the geomorphological significance of the Deccan Plateau. |
| 18 | Geography | Analyze the factors influencing the distribution of population in India. |
| 19 | Geography | Discuss the significance of ocean currents in influencing world climate. |
| 20 | Geography | Examine the causes of frequent flooding in the Brahmaputra basin. |
| 21 | Art & Culture | Discuss the contribution of the Bhakti and Sufi movements to Indian syncretic culture. |
| 22 | Society | Examine the challenges of communalism in contemporary India and suggest remedies. |

### G.1 Major Rivers of India (Origin, Length Rank, Mouth)

| River | Origin | Falls Into | Notable Fact |
|-------|--------|------------|---------------|
| Ganga | Gangotri Glacier | Bay of Bengal | Longest river in India (with Brahmaputra debated) |
| Indus | Near Mansarovar (Tibet) | Arabian Sea (Pakistan) | Gives India its name |
| Brahmaputra | Angsi Glacier (Tibet) | Bay of Bengal | Known as Yarlung Tsangpo in Tibet |
| Godavari | Trimbakeshwar, Maharashtra | Bay of Bengal | Called 'Dakshin Ganga' |
| Krishna | Mahabaleshwar, Maharashtra | Bay of Bengal | Second-longest Peninsular river |
| Kaveri | Talakaveri, Karnataka | Bay of Bengal | Subject of Cauvery water dispute |
| Narmada | Amarkantak, MP | Arabian Sea | Flows through a rift valley |
| Tapi | Multai, MP | Arabian Sea | Flows parallel to Narmada |
| Mahanadi | Sihawa, Chhattisgarh | Bay of Bengal | Hirakud Dam is built on it |
| Yamuna | Yamunotri Glacier | Confluences with Ganga at Prayagraj | Longest tributary of the Ganga |
| Chambal | Near Mhow, MP | Yamuna | Known for ravines and gharial sanctuary |
| Son | Amarkantak region | Ganga | Major south-bank tributary of Ganga |
| Beas | Beas Kund, HP | Sutlej | Associated with Alexander's retreat point |
| Sutlej | Rakas Lake (Tibet) | Indus (via Pakistan) | Easternmost of the 'five rivers' of Punjab |
| Jhelum | Verinag, J&K | Chenab | Flows through Srinagar/Wular Lake |
