## 38. Appendix I: Full-Length Prelims Mock Test (100 Qs)

A single-sitting 100-question General Studies Paper I style mock, mixing all subjects in realistic proportion. Answer key given inline for immediate self-check; treat each as a 2-minute-max question under timed conditions.

| Q# | Question | Answer |
|----|----------|--------|
| 1 | Who founded the Sarvodaya movement in India along with Vinoba Bhave's Bhoodan? | Jayaprakash Narayan (Bhoodan by Vinoba Bhave; Sarvodaya broader movement) |
| 2 | The 'Doctrine of Lapse' was used to annex which of these states: Satara, Jhansi, Nagpur? | All three |
| 3 | Which Mughal emperor shifted the capital to Fatehpur Sikri? | Akbar |
| 4 | The Servants of India Society was founded by? | Gopal Krishna Gokhale |
| 5 | Which Act first introduced separate electorates in India? | Indian Councils Act, 1909 (Morley-Minto Reforms) |
| 6 | The 'Drain of Wealth' theory was propounded by? | Dadabhai Naoroji |
| 7 | Which session of the INC first demanded 'Purna Swaraj'? | Lahore Session, 1929 |
| 8 | The Poona Pact (1932) was signed between Gandhi and? | B.R. Ambedkar |
| 9 | Who was the first Indian Governor-General of independent India? | C. Rajagopalachari |
| 10 | Which river forms the Sunderbans delta along with the Ganga? | Brahmaputra (via Meghna) |
| 11 | The Tropic of Cancer passes through how many Indian states? | 8 |
| 12 | Which is the southernmost point of mainland India? | Kanyakumari (Indira Point is southernmost of India, in Andaman & Nicobar) |
| 13 | 'Loktak Lake', the only floating lake, is in which state? | Manipur |
| 14 | Which Indian state has the longest coastline? | Gujarat |
| 15 | Barren Island, India's only active volcano, is in? | Andaman & Nicobar Islands |
| 16 | Which soil type is most prone to soil erosion in India? | Sandy/arid soils generally, but black soil is prone to erosion when uncultivated on slopes |
| 17 | Which Five Year Plan is known as 'Gadgil Yojana'? | Fourth Five Year Plan |
| 18 | Who chaired the Drafting Committee sub-committee on Fundamental Rights? | Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel (Advisory Committee); Ambedkar chaired Drafting Committee |
| 19 | Which Article empowers Parliament to form new states? | Article 3 |
| 20 | The concept of 'Directive Principles' being non-justiciable is stated in which Article? | Article 37 |
| 21 | Which body recommends the distribution of net proceeds of taxes between Centre and States? | Finance Commission |
| 22 | The 'Rajya Sabha' can never be dissolved because? | It is a permanent/continuing chamber (1/3rd retire every 2 years) |
| 23 | Money Bills are certified as such by? | The Speaker of Lok Sabha |
| 24 | Which writ is issued to prevent a lower court from exceeding jurisdiction? | Prohibition |
| 25 | Which writ literally means 'to have the body'? | Habeas Corpus |
| 26 | The National Human Rights Commission was established under which Act? | Protection of Human Rights Act, 1993 |
| 27 | India's first woman President was? | Pratibha Patil |
| 28 | The concept of 'Judicial Review' is explicitly enumerated in which Article? | Article 13 |
| 29 | Repo rate is the rate at which? | RBI lends short-term funds to commercial banks |
| 30 | Reverse repo rate is the rate at which? | RBI borrows funds from commercial banks |
| 31 | 'Stagflation' refers to a situation of? | High inflation with stagnant growth/high unemployment |
| 32 | Disguised unemployment is most prevalent in which sector in India? | Agriculture |
| 33 | The Phillips Curve depicts the relationship between? | Inflation and unemployment |
| 34 | 'Angel Tax' in India is levied on? | Investments in unlisted startups above fair market value |
| 35 | Which index measures the cost of a fixed basket of goods for industrial workers? | CPI-IW |
| 36 | The 'Laffer Curve' illustrates the relationship between? | Tax rates and tax revenue |
| 37 | Which committee recommended the Fiscal Responsibility framework in India? | The idea evolved via FRBM Act 2003 (based on earlier committee inputs) |
| 38 | India's first Special Economic Zone was set up at? | Kandla (as Export Processing Zone, later SEZ model) |
| 39 | 'Sunrise sector' PLI schemes commonly cover which industries? | Electronics, semiconductors, solar PV, textiles, etc. |
| 40 | Priority Sector Lending (PSL) norms in India apply to? | Scheduled commercial banks (agriculture, MSME, etc.) |
| 41 | Which greenhouse gas has the highest global warming potential among common ones (per unit mass)? | Nitrous oxide/Methane (both higher than CO2; N2O commonly cited highest among the three main ones) |
| 42 | The Montreal Protocol deals with? | Substances that deplete the ozone layer |
| 43 | 'Blue Carbon' refers to carbon captured by? | Coastal/marine ecosystems (mangroves, seagrass, etc.) |
| 44 | The Western Ghats Ecology Expert Panel is also known as? | Gadgil Committee |
| 45 | 'Project Cheetah' involves reintroduction of cheetahs from? | Namibia and South Africa |
| 46 | The Namami Gange programme targets the conservation of? | River Ganga |
| 47 | Which gas is primarily responsible for the 'ozone hole'? | Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs) |
| 48 | The Bonn Challenge relates to? | Global forest landscape restoration |
| 49 | 'Net Zero' target year announced by India (COP26) is? | 2070 |
| 50 | Which Indian mission focuses on solar energy under the National Action Plan on Climate Change? | Jawaharlal Nehru National Solar Mission |
| 51 | ISRO's spaceport is located at? | Sriharikota, Andhra Pradesh |
| 52 | 'Digital India' programme was launched in which year? | 2015 |
| 53 | UPI transactions are processed by which body? | NPCI |
| 54 | 5G spectrum auctions in India were conducted in? | 2022 |
| 55 | 'Aadhaar' is issued by which authority? | UIDAI (Unique Identification Authority of India) |
| 56 | Which Indian mission aims to map India's genetic diversity? | Genome India Project |
| 57 | 'Bharat Net' project aims to provide? | Broadband connectivity to Gram Panchayats |
| 58 | India's indigenous aircraft carrier is named? | INS Vikrant |
| 59 | 'Akash' and 'Astra' are examples of India's indigenous? | Missiles |
| 60 | 'Tejas' is an indigenous Indian? | Light Combat Aircraft |
| 61 | Which classical dance form originated in Tamil Nadu? | Bharatanatyam |
| 62 | Kathakali is the classical dance form of which state? | Kerala |
| 63 | Which classical dance form is associated with Manipur? | Manipuri |
| 64 | Odissi classical dance originated in? | Odisha |
| 65 | The Sattriya dance form is associated with which state? | Assam |
| 66 | Which Indian classical music tradition is prevalent in the south? | Carnatic music |
| 67 | Khajuraho Dance Festival showcases dances against the backdrop of? | Khajuraho temples, Madhya Pradesh |
| 68 | The Sun Temple at Konark is dedicated to? | Surya (Sun God) |
| 69 | The Brihadeeswarar Temple is an example of which architectural style? | Dravidian style |
| 70 | 'Warli' painting is a folk art tradition from which state? | Maharashtra |
| 71 | 'Madhubani' painting originated in which state? | Bihar |
| 72 | 'Pattachitra' painting tradition is associated with which states? | Odisha and West Bengal |
| 73 | The classical language status has been given to how many Indian languages (as commonly cited)? | Six (Tamil, Sanskrit, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam, Odia) — count may expand over time |
| 74 | Which is the oldest classical language of India (first to be notified)? | Tamil |
| 75 | GI (Geographical Indication) tag has been given to which Bihar fruit famously? | Shahi Litchi |
| 76 | 'Darjeeling Tea' GI tag belongs to which state? | West Bengal |
| 77 | 'Nagpur Orange' GI tag belongs to which state? | Maharashtra |
| 78 | Which is the largest Gompa (Buddhist monastery) in India? | Tawang Monastery, Arunachal Pradesh |
| 79 | Ajanta and Ellora Caves are located in which state? | Maharashtra |
| 80 | India's first UNESCO World Heritage Site (inscribed) includes which sites (1983 batch)? | Agra Fort, Ajanta Caves, Ellora Caves, Taj Mahal (all inscribed together in 1983) |
| 81 | 'Champaner-Pavagadh Archaeological Park' is located in? | Gujarat |
| 82 | The Great Living Chola Temples (UNESCO) are located mainly in which state? | Tamil Nadu |
| 83 | Which is the highest gallantry award in India? | Param Vir Chakra |
| 84 | Bharat Ratna is India's highest? | Civilian award |
| 85 | Which award recognizes excellence in sports and was renamed in 2021? | Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna (formerly Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna) |
| 86 | 'Padma Vibhushan' ranks at which position among Padma awards? | Second-highest (after Padma Vibhushan is actually the highest Padma award, above Padma Bhushan and Padma Shri) |
| 87 | Nobel Peace Prize awarded to an Indian in 2014 was won by? | Kailash Satyarthi |
| 88 | India's first Nobel laureate (1913) was? | Rabindranath Tagore |
| 89 | C.V. Raman won the Nobel Prize in which field? | Physics |
| 90 | Amartya Sen won the Nobel Prize in which field? | Economic Sciences |
| 91 | Which international grouping includes Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa? | BRICS |
| 92 | The Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO) India joined as a full member in which year? | 2017 |
| 93 | QUAD grouping includes India, USA, Japan and? | Australia |
| 94 | 'Malabar' is a naval exercise conducted among India, USA, Japan and? | Australia |
| 95 | 'Yudh Abhyas' is a bilateral military exercise between India and? | USA |
| 96 | 'Indra' is a bilateral military exercise between India and? | Russia |
| 97 | The McMahon Line demarcates the boundary between India and? | China (in the Arunachal Pradesh sector) |
| 98 | The Radcliffe Line demarcates the boundary between India and? | Pakistan and Bangladesh |
| 99 | The Durand Line is a boundary between Pakistan and? | Afghanistan |
| 100 | The Line of Actual Control (LAC) separates Indian and Chinese-held territory, while the Line of Control (LoC) separates India and? | Pakistan (in Jammu & Kashmir) |

## 44. Appendix O: Extended PYQ Bank — Additional 200 Practice Questions

Further rapid-fire practice rows across all GS subjects, formatted for quick daily revision (aim: 100 rows in under 20 minutes).

### O.1 History & Culture (40 Qs)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | Who wrote the 'Indica', an account of Mauryan India? | Megasthenes |
| 2 | The 'Hiuen Tsang' Chinese traveller visited India during whose reign? | Harshavardhana |
| 3 | Fa-Hien visited India during whose reign? | Chandragupta II |
| 4 | The 'Navaratnas' (nine gems) were part of whose court? | Chandragupta II (also loosely Akbar's court had similar concept) |
| 5 | Which Gupta ruler is credited with the 'Prayag Prashasti'? | Samudragupta (composed by Harishena) |
| 6 | The 'Iron Pillar' at Mehrauli belongs to which period? | Gupta period |
| 7 | Which dynasty built the Ellora Kailasa Temple? | Rashtrakuta dynasty |
| 8 | Al-Biruni, who wrote 'Tahqiq-i-Hind', accompanied which invader? | Mahmud of Ghazni |
| 9 | The 'Vijayanagara Empire' battle that led to its decline? | Battle of Talikota (1565) |
| 10 | Guru Nanak, founder of Sikhism, was born in which year? | 1469 |
| 11 | The Golden Temple at Amritsar was built under which Guru? | Guru Ram Das (site) / Guru Arjan Dev (temple completion) |
| 12 | The 'Anandpur Sahib Resolution' relates to which community's demands? | Sikh community (Akali Dal) |
| 13 | The First Battle of Panipat (1526) was fought between Babur and? | Ibrahim Lodi |
| 14 | The Second Battle of Panipat (1556) was fought between Akbar's forces and? | Hemu (Hemchandra Vikramaditya) |
| 15 | The Battle of Haldighati (1576) was fought between Akbar's forces and? | Maharana Pratap |
| 16 | The Treaty of Purandar (1665) was signed between Shivaji and? | Mughals (Jai Singh I) |
| 17 | The Battle of Plassey (1757) was fought between the British and? | Siraj-ud-Daulah (Nawab of Bengal) |
| 18 | Robert Clive's key military role was demonstrated first at? | Battle of Arcot (1751) |
| 19 | The Treaty of Allahabad (1765) granted the British which right? | Diwani rights over Bengal, Bihar, Odisha |
| 20 | Warren Hastings faced impeachment proceedings led by? | Edmund Burke |
| 21 | The 'Pitt's India Act' (1784) established which body? | Board of Control |
| 22 | The Vernacular Press Act (1878) aimed to? | Curb Indian-language press criticism of British rule |
| 23 | The Ilbert Bill controversy (1883) related to? | Jurisdiction of Indian judges over Europeans |
| 24 | The Indian Association was founded by? | Surendranath Banerjee |
| 25 | The 'Deccan Education Society' was founded by? | Bal Gangadhar Tilak and others (1884) |
| 26 | The 'Ghadar Party' was founded in which country? | USA (1913) |
| 27 | The Komagata Maru incident (1914) involved passengers denied entry into? | Canada |
| 28 | The Champaran Satyagraha addressed grievances of which crop's cultivators? | Indigo |
| 29 | The Kheda Satyagraha addressed which grievance? | Tax relief for famine-hit peasants |
| 30 | The Ahmedabad Mill Strike (1918) was Gandhi's intervention in a dispute over? | Workers' wages/bonus |
| 31 | The Khilafat Movement was linked with the fate of the? | Ottoman Caliph |
| 32 | The Chauri Chaura incident (1922) led to withdrawal of? | Non-Cooperation Movement |
| 33 | The Swaraj Party was founded by? | C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru |
| 34 | The Nehru Report (1928) was a response to demand from? | Simon Commission (for Indians to draft their own constitution) |
| 35 | The 'Salt Satyagraha' began with Gandhi's march from Sabarmati to? | Dandi |
| 36 | The Government of India Act 1935 provided for a federation that never fully materialized due to? | Princely states not joining |
| 37 | The 'August Offer' (1940) was made by which Viceroy? | Lord Linlithgow |
| 38 | The Cripps Mission (1942) was rejected mainly over the issue of? | Immediate transfer of full power / minority safeguards |
| 39 | The 'Direct Action Day' (1946) was called by? | Muslim League |
| 40 | The Mountbatten Plan (June 1947) proposed? | Partition of India into two dominions |

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | Which article guarantees equality before law? | Article 14 |
| 2 | Which article prohibits discrimination on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth? | Article 15 |

| 10 | Which article guarantees freedom of conscience and religion? | Article 25 |
| 11 | Which article guarantees freedom to manage religious affairs? | Article 26 |
| 12 | Which article protects cultural and educational rights of minorities? | Article 29-30 |
| 13 | Which article deals with the Union Judiciary? | Article 124 |
| 14 | Which article establishes the Comptroller and Auditor General? | Article 148 |
| 15 | Which article establishes the Union Public Service Commission? | Article 315 |
| 16 | Which article deals with the composition of the Council of Ministers? | Article 74/75 |
| 17 | Which article deals with the Attorney General for India? | Article 76 |
| 18 | Which article empowers the President to promulgate ordinances? | Article 123 |
| 19 | Which article empowers Governors to promulgate ordinances? | Article 213 |
| 20 | Which article specifies the composition of the Rajya Sabha? | Article 80 |
| 21 | Which article specifies the composition of the Lok Sabha? | Article 81 |
| 22 | Which article deals with the Union Budget (Annual Financial Statement)? | Article 112 |
| 23 | Which article deals with the Contingency Fund of India? | Article 267 |
| 24 | Which article empowers the creation of All India Services? | Article 312 |
| 25 | Which article deals with special provisions for Delhi (NCT)? | Article 239AA |
| 26 | Which article deals with the Inter-State Council? | Article 263 |
| 27 | Which schedule lists the Union, State, and Concurrent Lists? | Seventh Schedule |
| 28 | Which schedule deals with allocation of seats in the Rajya Sabha? | Fourth Schedule |
| 29 | Which schedule contains forms of oaths/affirmations? | Third Schedule |
| 30 | Which schedule lists names of States and Union Territories? | First Schedule |
| 31 | Who administers the oath of office to the President? | Chief Justice of India |
| 32 | Who administers the oath of office to the Prime Minister? | President |
| 33 | The Speaker of Lok Sabha is elected by? | Members of the Lok Sabha |
| 34 | The Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha is elected by? | Members of the Rajya Sabha |
| 35 | Who presides over a joint sitting of both Houses of Parliament? | Speaker of Lok Sabha |
| 36 | The quorum for a Parliament sitting is what fraction of total members? | 1/10th |
| 37 | A No-Confidence Motion can be moved only in which House? | Lok Sabha |
| 38 | The 'Question Hour' is typically held at the start of which parliamentary session period? | First hour of a sitting |
| 39 | 'Guillotine' in parliamentary procedure refers to? | Putting all remaining demands to vote without discussion due to time constraints |
| 40 | The Union Council of Ministers is collectively responsible to? | The Lok Sabha |

### O.3 Economy, Environment & Science (40 Qs)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | GVA stands for? | Gross Value Added |
| 2 | 'Twin Balance Sheet' problem refers to stress in which two sectors? | Banks and corporates |
| 3 | 'Core inflation' excludes which volatile components? | Food and fuel |
| 4 | 'Green GDP' attempts to account for? | Environmental costs/degradation in economic output |
| 5 | The 'informal sector' in India is best characterized by? | Lack of regulation/social security coverage |
| 6 | 'Universal Basic Income' is a proposal for? | Unconditional cash transfer to all citizens |
| 7 | 'Demographic dividend' refers to the benefit from? | A large working-age population share |
| 8 | India's 'demographic dividend' window is generally estimated to last until around? | 2040s (varies by estimate) |
| 9 | 'Current Account Deficit' occurs when? | Imports of goods/services/income exceed exports |
| 10 | 'Capital Account Convertibility' refers to? | Free conversion of domestic financial assets into foreign ones |
| 11 | The 'Multidimensional Poverty Index' is released by? | NITI Aayog (India-specific) / UNDP-OPHI (global) |
| 12 | 'Green Bonds' are used to finance? | Environmentally sustainable projects |
| 13 | 'Carbon Trading' mechanism allows? | Buying/selling of emission credits |
| 14 | 'Just Transition' in climate policy refers to? | Shifting to green economy while protecting affected workers/communities |
| 15 | 'Ocean acidification' is primarily caused by? | Absorption of excess atmospheric CO2 by oceans |
| 16 | 'Eutrophication' of water bodies is caused mainly by? | Excess nutrient (nitrogen/phosphorus) runoff |
| 17 | 'Bioremediation' refers to? | Use of organisms to clean up pollutants |
| 18 | 'Ecological footprint' measures? | Human demand on nature's resources vs. regeneration capacity |
| 19 | 'Keystone species' is defined as? | A species with disproportionately large effect on its ecosystem |
| 20 | 'Endemic species' refers to? | Species found only in a specific geographic area |
| 21 | 'In-situ conservation' example? | National Parks, Wildlife Sanctuaries |
| 22 | 'Ex-situ conservation' example? | Zoos, seed banks, botanical gardens |
| 23 | 'Red Data Book' is maintained by? | IUCN |
| 24 | 'Coral bleaching' is primarily caused by? | Rising sea temperatures (thermal stress) |
| 25 | 'Genetically Modified Organism' (GMO) definition? | Organism whose genetic material has been altered using biotechnology |
| 26 | 'Stem cell therapy' potential use? | Regenerating damaged tissues/organs |
| 27 | 'Nanomedicine' refers to? | Application of nanotechnology in medical diagnosis/treatment |
| 28 | 'Quantum entanglement' is a phenomenon where? | Particles' states remain correlated regardless of distance |
| 29 | 'Dark matter' is inferred to exist because of? | Its gravitational effects, despite not emitting light |
| 30 | 'Exoplanet' refers to? | A planet outside our solar system |
| 31 | 'CRISPR-Cas9' functions primarily as a? | Gene-editing tool |
| 32 | 'Herd immunity' is achieved when? | A sufficient proportion of a population becomes immune to a disease |
| 33 | 'Antimicrobial resistance' (AMR) is a growing concern because? | Reduces effectiveness of drugs against infections |
| 34 | 'Telemedicine' refers to? | Remote clinical healthcare delivery via technology |
| 35 | 'Digital divide' refers to? | Gap in access to digital technology between groups |
| 36 | 'Net metering' in solar policy allows? | Consumers to sell excess solar power back to the grid |
| 37 | 'Green Hydrogen' is produced using? | Electrolysis powered by renewable energy |
| 38 | 'Circular economy' model emphasizes? | Reduce, reuse, recycle over a linear take-make-dispose model |
| 39 | 'E-waste' management rules in India were notified under which Act's framework? | Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 |
| 40 | 'Extended Producer Responsibility' (EPR) requires manufacturers to? | Manage the post-consumer stage of a product's life cycle |

### O.4 Ethics & Miscellaneous (30 Qs)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | 'Deontological ethics' judges actions based on? | Adherence to rules/duties, not consequences |
| 2 | 'Consequentialist ethics' (e.g., utilitarianism) judges actions based on? | Their outcomes/consequences |
| 3 | 'Virtue ethics' focuses primarily on? | Character and moral virtues of the person |
| 4 | 'Cognitive dissonance' refers to? | Mental discomfort from holding contradictory beliefs/values |
| 5 | 'Whistleblower' refers to? | A person exposing wrongdoing within an organization |
| 6 | The 'Whistle Blowers Protection Act' in India was enacted in? | 2014 |
| 7 | 'Nolan Committee' (UK) principles relate to? | Standards/principles for public life |
| 8 | 'Conflict of interest' in public service refers to? | Personal interest clashing with official duty |
| 9 | 'Nudge theory' in public policy refers to? | Indirect suggestions to influence behaviour without mandates |
| 10 | 'Citizen's Charter' aims to? | Specify service standards/commitments to citizens |
| 11 | 'Social audit' involves? | Community-level scrutiny of public programme implementation |
| 12 | 'RTI Act' empowers citizens to? | Seek information from public authorities |
| 13 | 'e-governance' 'G2C' model refers to? | Government-to-Citizen service delivery |
| 14 | 'Good governance' indicators typically include? | Accountability, transparency, responsiveness, rule of law |
| 15 | 'Devolution of power' strengthens which democratic principle? | Subsidiarity/grassroots democracy |
| 16 | 'Moral courage' in civil service refers to? | Standing by ethical decisions despite pressure/risk |
| 17 | 'Empathy' in administration helps primarily with? | Understanding citizens'/beneficiaries' perspectives |
| 18 | 'Objectivity' as a civil service value means? | Basing decisions on merit, free of bias |
| 19 | 'Non-partisanship' expected of civil servants means? | Serving impartially regardless of ruling party |
| 20 | 'Transparency' as a governance value primarily reduces? | Corruption and information asymmetry |
| 21 | 'Rule of law' principle implies? | No one is above the law; equal application of law |
| 22 | 'Public interest' as a criterion for decision-making prioritizes? | Collective welfare over narrow/personal interest |
| 23 | 'Ethical dilemma' is best defined as? | A situation requiring choice between conflicting moral principles |
| 24 | 'Perception' in attitude formation refers to? | The process of interpreting sensory information |
| 25 | 'Values' are best defined as? | Enduring beliefs about what is desirable/important |
| 26 | 'Aptitude' for civil services refers to? | Natural or acquired capability/inclination for public service |
| 27 | 'Corporate Social Responsibility' (CSR) in India is mandated under which Act? | Companies Act, 2013 |
| 28 | 'Lokayukta' functions at which level of government? | State level (state ombudsman) |
| 29 | 'Right to Public Services' Acts (state-level) aim to guarantee? | Time-bound delivery of public services |
| 30 | 'Emotional labour' in public-facing jobs refers to? | Managing emotions to meet job/role expectations |

## 46. Appendix Q: Complete Constitutional Amendments Chronology

| Amendment | Year | Key Provision |
|-----------|------|-----------------|
| 1st | 1951 | Added Ninth Schedule; reasonable restrictions on speech; land reform protection |
| 4th | 1955 | Restricted judicial review of compensation in property acquisition laws |
| 7th | 1956 | States Reorganisation (linguistic basis) |
| 9th | 1960 | Transferred Berubari Union territory to Pakistan |
| 10th | 1961 | Incorporated Dadra and Nagar Haveli into the Union |
| 12th | 1962 | Incorporated Goa, Daman and Diu into the Union |
| 13th | 1962 | Formed Nagaland as a state |
| 14th | 1962 | Incorporated Puducherry as a Union Territory |
| 18th | 1966 | Reorganized Punjab (creation of Haryana, Himachal Pradesh) |
| 21st | 1969 | Added Sindhi language to the Eighth Schedule |
| 24th | 1971 | Affirmed Parliament's power to amend any part of the Constitution, including Fundamental Rights |
| 25th | 1971 | Curtailed right to property; introduced Article 31C |
| 26th | 1971 | Abolished privy purses of former princely rulers |
| 31st | 1973 | Increased Lok Sabha seats |
| 36th | 1975 | Made Sikkim a full state of the Union |
| 38th | 1975 | Made President's satisfaction in declaring Emergency final and non-justiciable |
| 39th | 1975 | Kept election disputes of PM/Speaker outside judicial review (Emergency-era) |
| 42nd | 1976 | 'Mini-Constitution' — added Socialist, Secular, Integrity to Preamble; added Fundamental Duties; curtailed judicial review |
| 44th | 1978 | Removed Right to Property from Fundamental Rights (made a legal right, Art 300A); restored safeguards diluted by 42nd |
| 52nd | 1985 | Anti-defection law (Tenth Schedule) |
| 61st | 1989 | Reduced voting age from 21 to 18 |
| 65th | 1990 | Established National Commission for SCs/STs |
| 69th | 1991 | Gave Delhi special status as National Capital Territory (NCT) |
| 71st | 1992 | Added Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali to Eighth Schedule |
| 73rd | 1992 | Institutionalized Panchayati Raj (rural local self-government) |
| 74th | 1992 | Institutionalized Urban Local Bodies (Nagarpalikas) |
| 86th | 2002 | Made education a Fundamental Right (Article 21A) for children 6-14 |
| 91st | 2003 | Limited Council of Ministers' size; strengthened anti-defection provisions |
| 92nd | 2003 | Added Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali to Eighth Schedule |
| 97th | 2011 | Made the right to form cooperative societies a Fundamental Right |
| 100th | 2015 | Enabled India-Bangladesh Land Boundary Agreement (exchange of enclaves) |
| 101st | 2016 | Introduced the Goods and Services Tax (GST) |
| 102nd | 2018 | Gave constitutional status to the National Commission for Backward Classes |
| 103rd | 2019 | Introduced 10% EWS (Economically Weaker Sections) reservation |
| 104th | 2020 | Extended SC/ST reservation in Lok Sabha/Assemblies; removed Anglo-Indian nominated seats |
| 105th | 2021 | Restored states' power to identify their own Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (SEBCs) |
| 106th | 2023 | Reserved one-third of seats for women in Lok Sabha, State Assemblies, and Delhi Assembly (Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam) |

## 47. Appendix R: Master Chronology of Indian History (Ancient to Modern)

A single continuous timeline spanning the entire History syllabus — ideal for a final quick revision pass before Prelims.

| Year/Period | Event |
|---------------|-------|
| c. 2500 BCE | Mature phase of Indus Valley Civilization |
| c. 1500 BCE | Approximate start of the Vedic Age |
| 599 BCE (approx.) | Birth of Mahavira |
| 563 BCE (approx.) | Birth of Gautama Buddha |
| 326 BCE | Alexander's invasion of India (Battle of Hydaspes) |
| 322 BCE | Founding of the Mauryan Empire by Chandragupta Maurya |
| 261 BCE | Kalinga War fought by Ashoka |
| 185 BCE | Fall of the Mauryan Empire |
| 78 CE | Beginning of the Saka Era (Kanishka's reign, approx.) |
| 320 CE | Founding of the Gupta Empire by Chandragupta I |
| 375-415 CE | Reign of Chandragupta II 'Vikramaditya' |
| 606-647 CE | Reign of Harshavardhana |
| 712 CE | Arab conquest of Sindh (Muhammad bin Qasim) |
| 985-1014 CE | Reign of Rajaraja Chola I |
| 1000-1027 CE | Repeated raids of Mahmud of Ghazni into India |
| 1191-1192 CE | Battles of Tarain (Prithviraj Chauhan vs Muhammad Ghori) |
| 1206 CE | Founding of the Delhi Sultanate (Qutb-ud-din Aibak) |
| 1290-1320 CE | Khalji dynasty (incl. Alauddin Khalji's reign) |
| 1320-1414 CE | Tughlaq dynasty (incl. Muhammad bin Tughlaq's experiments) |
| 1336 CE | Founding of the Vijayanagara Empire |
| 1347 CE | Founding of the Bahmani Kingdom |
| 1398 CE | Timur's invasion of Delhi |
| 1469 CE | Birth of Guru Nanak |
| 1498 CE | Vasco da Gama's arrival at Calicut |
| 1526 CE | First Battle of Panipat; founding of the Mughal Empire (Babur) |
| 1540-1555 CE | Sur interregnum (Sher Shah Suri's reign) |
| 1556 CE | Second Battle of Panipat (Akbar vs Hemu) |
| 1556-1605 CE | Reign of Akbar |
| 1565 CE | Battle of Talikota — decline of Vijayanagara Empire |
| 1576 CE | Battle of Haldighati (Maharana Pratap vs Mughals) |
| 1600 CE | Founding of the English East India Company |
| 1605-1627 CE | Reign of Jahangir |
| 1628-1658 CE | Reign of Shah Jahan (Taj Mahal built) |
| 1658-1707 CE | Reign of Aurangzeb |
| 1674 CE | Coronation of Shivaji as Chhatrapati |
| 1707 CE | Death of Aurangzeb — beginning of Mughal decline |
| 1739 CE | Nadir Shah's invasion and sack of Delhi |
| 1757 CE | Battle of Plassey |
| 1761 CE | Third Battle of Panipat (Marathas vs Ahmad Shah Abdali) |
| 1764 CE | Battle of Buxar |
| 1765 CE | Treaty of Allahabad — East India Company gets Diwani rights |
| 1773 CE | Regulating Act passed by British Parliament |
| 1784 CE | Pitt's India Act |
| 1793 CE | Permanent Settlement of Bengal introduced |
| 1829 CE | Abolition of Sati (Lord William Bentinck) |
| 1835 CE | English Education Act (Macaulay's Minute) |
| 1853 CE | First railway line opened (Bombay to Thane) |
| 1857 CE | Revolt of 1857 |
| 1858 CE | Government of India Act — Crown takes direct control |
| 1861 CE | Indian Councils Act |
| 1885 CE | Founding of the Indian National Congress |
| 1905 CE | Partition of Bengal |
| 1906 CE | Founding of the All India Muslim League |
| 1909 CE | Morley-Minto Reforms (Indian Councils Act) |
| 1911 CE | Annulment of Bengal partition; capital shifted to Delhi |
| 1913 CE | Founding of the Ghadar Party |
| 1916 CE | Lucknow Pact (Congress-Muslim League unity) |
| 1917 CE | Champaran Satyagraha |
| 1919 CE | Rowlatt Act; Jallianwala Bagh Massacre; Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms |
| 1920-22 CE | Non-Cooperation Movement (ends after Chauri Chaura, 1922) |
| 1928 CE | Simon Commission visit; Nehru Report |
| 1929 CE | Lahore Session — 'Purna Swaraj' declared as the goal |
| 1930 CE | Dandi March; Civil Disobedience Movement begins |
| 1930-32 CE | Round Table Conferences (three sessions) |
| 1932 CE | Communal Award; Poona Pact |
| 1935 CE | Government of India Act (provincial autonomy) |
| 1939 CE | Congress ministries resign (WWII, without consultation) |
| 1940 CE | 'August Offer' by Lord Linlithgow; Lahore Resolution (Muslim League) |
| 1942 CE | Cripps Mission; Quit India Movement |
| 1943 CE | INA under Subhas Chandra Bose reorganized; Bengal Famine |
| 1945 CE | INA (Red Fort) Trials begin |
| 1946 CE | Cabinet Mission Plan; Direct Action Day; Interim Government formed |
| 1947 CE | Independence and Partition of India |
| 1949 CE | Constitution of India adopted (26 November) |
| 1950 CE | Constitution of India comes into force (26 January) |
| 1951-52 CE | First General Elections held |
| 1956 CE | States Reorganisation Act (linguistic states) |
| 1961 CE | Liberation of Goa from Portuguese rule |
| 1962 CE | Sino-Indian War |
| 1965 CE | Second Indo-Pak War |
| 1966 CE | Tashkent Agreement |
| 1971 CE | Third Indo-Pak War; Liberation of Bangladesh |
| 1974 CE | Pokhran-I nuclear test ('Smiling Buddha') |
| 1975-77 CE | The Emergency |
| 1991 CE | Balance of payments crisis; Economic Liberalization (LPG reforms) begin |
| 1992 CE | Babri Masjid demolition |
| 1998 CE | Pokhran-II nuclear tests |
| 1999 CE | Kargil War |
| 2000 CE | Formation of three new states — Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand |
| 2005 CE | Right to Information Act passed |
| 2014 CE | Formation of Telangana (29th state, since reorganized to current structure) |
| 2016 CE | Demonetization; GST Constitutional Amendment passed |
| 2017 CE | GST implemented nationwide |
| 2019 CE | Abrogation of Article 370's special provisions for J&K; state reorganized into two UTs |
| 2020 CE | Farm Laws passed (later repealed in 2021); COVID-19 pandemic response |
| 2023 CE | Chandrayaan-3 successful Moon landing; Women's Reservation Act passed |

## 51. Appendix V: Indian Regulatory, Investigative & Constitutional Bodies

### V.1 Key Regulatory Bodies

| Body | Regulates | Nature |
|------|-----------|--------|
| Reserve Bank of India (RBI) | Banking and monetary policy | Statutory |
| Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) | Securities markets | Statutory |
| Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority (IRDAI) | Insurance sector | Statutory |
| Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority (PFRDA) | Pension funds | Statutory |
| Telecom Regulatory Authority of India (TRAI) | Telecommunications | Statutory |
| Competition Commission of India (CCI) | Market competition/anti-trust | Statutory |
| Central Electricity Regulatory Commission (CERC) | Electricity sector (inter-state) | Statutory |
| Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) | Food safety standards | Statutory |
| Airports Economic Regulatory Authority (AERA) | Airport tariffs | Statutory |
| National Green Tribunal (NGT) | Environmental disputes | Statutory tribunal |

### V.2 Key Investigative & Anti-Corruption Bodies

| Body | Mandate |
|------|---------|
| Central Bureau of Investigation (CBI) | Investigates corruption, economic offences, major crimes |
| Enforcement Directorate (ED) | Enforces economic/foreign exchange laws (PMLA, FEMA) |
| National Investigation Agency (NIA) | Investigates terrorism and related offences |
| Central Vigilance Commission (CVC) | Oversees vigilance/anti-corruption in central government |
| Serious Fraud Investigation Office (SFIO) | Investigates corporate fraud |
| Narcotics Control Bureau (NCB) | Enforces narcotics/drug laws |
| Central Economic Intelligence Bureau (CEIB) | Coordinates economic intelligence agencies |
| Directorate of Revenue Intelligence (DRI) | Anti-smuggling and customs intelligence |

### V.3 Key Constitutional & Statutory Bodies

| Body | Constitutional/Statutory | Key Function |
|------|-----------------------------|-----------------|
| Election Commission of India | Constitutional | Conducts free and fair elections |
| Union Public Service Commission | Constitutional | Recruitment to All India/Central Services |
| Finance Commission | Constitutional | Centre-State tax devolution recommendations |
| Comptroller and Auditor General | Constitutional | Audits government accounts/expenditure |
| National Commission for SCs | Constitutional | Safeguards for Scheduled Castes |
| National Commission for STs | Constitutional | Safeguards for Scheduled Tribes |
| National Commission for Backward Classes | Constitutional (post-102nd amendment) | Safeguards for backward classes |
| National Human Rights Commission | Statutory | Protection of human rights |
| National Commission for Women | Statutory | Safeguards for women's rights |
| Lokpal | Statutory | Anti-corruption ombudsman at Union level |
| National Commission for Protection of Child Rights | Statutory | Safeguards for children's rights |
| Central Information Commission | Statutory | Adjudicates RTI-related appeals/complaints |

|----------------------|------|---------|

| Treaty of Versailles | 1919 | Ended World War I |
| Kyoto Protocol | 1997 | Greenhouse gas emission reduction commitments |
| Paris Agreement | 2015 | Global climate action framework |
| Montreal Protocol | 1987 | Phasing out ozone-depleting substances |
| Basel Convention | 1989 | Control of transboundary hazardous waste movement |
| Rotterdam Convention | 1998 | Prior informed consent for hazardous chemicals trade |
| Stockholm Convention | 2001 | Elimination of persistent organic pollutants |
| Ramsar Convention | 1971 | Conservation of wetlands |
| Convention on Biological Diversity | 1992 | Conservation of biodiversity |
| CITES | 1973 | Regulating trade in endangered species |
| UNCLOS | 1982 | Law of the Sea (maritime boundaries, EEZ, etc.) |
| Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty (NPT) | 1968 | Preventing spread of nuclear weapons (India not a signatory) |
| Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty (CTBT) | 1996 | Banning all nuclear test explosions (India not a signatory) |
| Chemical Weapons Convention | 1993 | Banning chemical weapons production/use |
| Biological Weapons Convention | 1972 | Banning biological weapons |
| Indus Waters Treaty | 1960 | Water sharing between India and Pakistan |
| Simla Agreement | 1972 | India-Pakistan post-1971 war framework, established LoC |
| Tashkent Agreement | 1966 | India-Pakistan post-1965 war agreement |
| India-Bangladesh Land Boundary Agreement | 1974 (ratified 2015) | Exchange of border enclaves |
| SAFTA (South Asian Free Trade Area) | 2004 | Regional free trade among SAARC nations |
| RCEP (Regional Comprehensive Economic Partnership) | 2020 | Asia-Pacific trade bloc (India opted out) |
| Comprehensive Economic Partnership Agreements (various) | Ongoing | India's bilateral trade agreements (e.g., UAE, Australia) |
| Artemis Accords | 2020 | Framework for international lunar/space exploration cooperation |
| Outer Space Treaty | 1967 | Governs activities in outer space |
| Antarctic Treaty | 1959 | Preserves Antarctica for peaceful/scientific purposes |
| Geneva Conventions | 1949 | International humanitarian law (rules of war) |
| Universal Declaration of Human Rights | 1948 | Foundational international human rights document |
| Vienna Convention on Diplomatic Relations | 1961 | Governs diplomatic immunity/relations |
| Minamata Convention on Mercury | 2013 | Protecting health/environment from mercury emissions |

## 62. Appendix AG: Additional Full-Length Mains Model Answers

Five more fully worked model answers, one per major paper, each structured with the intro-body-conclusion skeleton from Section 16. Use these as templates to reverse-engineer your own answers for similar questions.

### AG.1 GS1 — "Discuss the socio-economic impact of the Green Revolution on Indian agriculture." (15 marks, ~250 words)

**Introduction:**
- The Green Revolution (mid-1960s onward) introduced high-yielding variety (HYV) seeds, chemical fertilizers, irrigation expansion, and mechanization, transforming Indian agriculture from a food-deficit to a food-surplus economy.

**Body — Positive Impacts:**
- Food security: India moved from ship-to-mouth dependency to becoming a net foodgrain exporter over subsequent decades.
- Farmer incomes: Early-adopting regions (Punjab, Haryana, western UP) saw substantial rises in farm incomes and land values.
- Buffer stock capacity: Enabled the Food Corporation of India to build strategic reserves for price stabilization and PDS supply.
- Allied industrial growth: Boosted demand for fertilizers, farm machinery, and rural credit institutions.

**Body — Negative/Structural Impacts:**
- Regional disparity: Benefits concentrated in irrigated northwestern states, widening the gap with rain-fed eastern and central India.
- Ecological stress: Overuse of groundwater, soil salinization, and fertilizer-driven soil degradation in Green Revolution belts.
- Crop monoculture: Overemphasis on wheat and rice reduced cropping diversity and traditional millet cultivation.
- Farmer distress: Rising input costs and stagnant MSP-driven returns have contributed to indebtedness in later decades.

**Conclusion:**
- A "Second Green Revolution," focused on pulses, oilseeds, eastern India, and sustainable water use, is now widely advocated to correct these imbalances while preserving the original food-security gains.

### AG.2 GS2 — "Examine the role of the Election Commission of India in strengthening electoral democracy." (15 marks, ~250 words)

**Introduction:**
- The Election Commission of India (ECI), a permanent constitutional body under Article 324, is entrusted with superintendence, direction, and control of elections to Parliament, state legislatures, and the offices of President and Vice-President.

**Body — Key Contributions:**
- Free and fair elections: Enforcement of the Model Code of Conduct curbs the misuse of official machinery and money power during campaigns.
- Electoral reforms: Introduction of EVMs and VVPATs has reduced booth capturing and improved counting transparency.
- Voter awareness: Systematic Voters' Education and Electoral Participation (SVEEP) initiatives have improved voter turnout and awareness.
- Institutional independence: Security of tenure for the Chief Election Commissioner insulates the body from executive pressure.

**Body — Persistent Challenges:**
- Money and muscle power still influence outcomes in several constituencies despite expenditure ceilings.
- Criminalization of politics remains inadequately addressed through ECI mechanisms alone.
- Simultaneous elections ('One Nation, One Election') proposals raise federalism-related concerns still under debate.

**Conclusion:**
- Continuous strengthening of ECI's autonomy, technology adoption, and coordination with judicial and legislative reforms remains central to deepening India's electoral democracy.

### AG.3 GS3 — "Discuss the significance of digital public infrastructure (DPI) for India's economic development." (15 marks, ~250 words)

**Introduction:**
- Digital Public Infrastructure — comprising Aadhaar, UPI, and DigiLocker among others (the 'India Stack') — refers to foundational, interoperable digital systems that enable public and private service delivery at population scale.

**Body — Economic Significance:**
- Financial inclusion: Aadhaar-linked bank accounts combined with UPI have brought unprecedented numbers of citizens into the formal financial system.
- Reduced transaction costs: Direct Benefit Transfers eliminate middlemen and leakages in subsidy delivery.
- MSME formalization: Digital payment trails help small businesses access formal credit based on transaction history.
- Global template: India's DPI model is being studied and adopted by other developing nations (G20 DPI advocacy).

**Body — Challenges:**
- Digital divide: Rural and elderly populations face access and literacy barriers.
- Data privacy: Concerns around surveillance and data protection require robust legal safeguards (Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023).
- Cybersecurity: Population-scale systems are high-value targets for fraud and breaches.

**Conclusion:**
- DPI represents a genuinely Indian innovation in governance technology, but its long-term success depends on parallel investment in digital literacy and data protection safeguards.

### AG.4 GS4 — "What do you understand by 'public accountability'? Discuss its significance for good governance." (10 marks, ~150 words)

**Introduction:**
- Public accountability refers to the obligation of public officials and institutions to answer for their decisions and actions to citizens and oversight bodies.

**Body:**
- It ensures the responsible use of public resources and prevents arbitrary exercise of power.
- Mechanisms include legislative oversight (PAC, committees), audits (CAG), judicial review, and citizen tools like RTI and social audits.
- It builds public trust and legitimizes government action in a democracy.
- Weak accountability enables corruption, policy capture, and erosion of institutional credibility.

**Conclusion:**
- Strengthening accountability mechanisms — institutional, legal, and social — is foundational to translating democratic mandates into responsive, ethical governance.

### AG.5 Essay Skeleton — "Technology as a double-edged sword" (Full essay outline, not full prose)

- **Opening anecdote/quote:** A short illustrative example of technology solving and simultaneously creating a problem (e.g., social media enabling connection while fuelling misinformation).
- **Dimension 1 — Economic:** Automation boosts productivity but threatens low-skill employment.
- **Dimension 2 — Social:** Digital connectivity reduces distance but deepens the digital divide and mental health concerns.
- **Dimension 3 — Political:** E-governance improves service delivery but enables surveillance risks.
- **Dimension 4 — Environmental:** Clean-tech innovation aids sustainability, but e-waste and energy-hungry data centres add new burdens.
- **Balancing view:** Technology itself is neutral; outcomes depend on the regulatory and ethical frameworks societies build around it.
- **Conclusion:** A future-ready society must pair technological adoption with proactive governance, ethics, and inclusion safeguards.

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | 'Liquidity trap' refers to a situation where? | Monetary policy becomes ineffective at near-zero interest rates |
| 2 | 'Crowding out effect' refers to? | Government borrowing reducing private investment |
| 3 | 'Twin deficit' refers to simultaneous? | Fiscal deficit and current account deficit |
| 4 | 'Base erosion and profit shifting' (BEPS) relates to? | Multinational tax avoidance strategies |
| 5 | 'Transfer pricing' issues arise in taxation of? | Intra-group transactions of multinational companies |
| 6 | 'Angel tax' controversy primarily affected? | Startups raising funds from investors |
| 7 | 'Disinvestment' refers to? | Government selling stake in public sector enterprises |
| 8 | 'Strategic disinvestment' differs from minority stake sale by? | Involving transfer of management control |
| 9 | 'National Monetization Pipeline' involves? | Monetizing brownfield public infrastructure assets |
| 10 | 'Bad Bank' (NARCL) concept in India aims to? | Consolidate and resolve stressed bank assets |
| 11 | 'Basel III norms' relate to? | International banking capital adequacy standards |
| 12 | 'CAMELS rating' is used by regulators to assess? | Bank soundness (Capital, Assets, Management, Earnings, Liquidity, Sensitivity) |
| 13 | 'Systemically Important Banks' (D-SIBs) in India include? | SBI, HDFC Bank, ICICI Bank (as commonly designated) |
| 14 | 'Shadow banking' refers to? | Non-bank financial intermediaries performing bank-like functions |
| 15 | 'Microfinance institutions' primarily serve? | Low-income/unbanked borrowers with small loans |
| 16 | 'Financial Stability and Development Council' (FSDC) is chaired by? | Union Finance Minister |
| 17 | 'Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India' (IBBI) regulates? | Insolvency resolution processes |
| 18 | 'National Company Law Tribunal' (NCLT) adjudicates? | Corporate insolvency and company law matters |
| 19 | 'Ease of Living Index' (India-specific) measures? | Quality of life across Indian cities |
| 20 | 'Sustainable Development Goals' (SDGs) total number? | 17 |
| 21 | 'SDG India Index' is published by? | NITI Aayog |
| 22 | 'Antyodaya' concept, meaning 'upliftment of the last person', is associated with? | Deendayal Upadhyaya's Integral Humanism philosophy |
| 23 | 'Sarvodaya' concept is associated with? | Mahatma Gandhi (and later Vinoba Bhave) |
| 24 | 'Trusteeship theory' relating to wealth was propounded by? | Mahatma Gandhi |
| 25 | 'Panchayati Raj' three-tier structure comprises? | Gram Panchayat, Panchayat Samiti (Block), Zila Parishad (District) |

### AH.4 Science, Space & Miscellaneous (25 Qs)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | 'Photosynthesis' converts light energy into? | Chemical energy (glucose) |
| 2 | 'Chlorophyll' is essential for absorbing which light wavelengths mainly? | Red and blue light (reflects green) |
| 3 | 'Mitochondria' is known as the 'powerhouse' of the? | Cell |
| 4 | 'DNA' stands for? | Deoxyribonucleic Acid |
| 5 | 'RNA' differs structurally from DNA by being? | Single-stranded (and using ribose sugar/uracil) |
| 6 | 'Vaccination' works on the principle of building? | Immunological memory |
| 7 | 'Antibodies' are produced by which type of cell? | B-lymphocytes (plasma cells) |
| 8 | 'Insulin' is produced by which organ? | Pancreas |
| 9 | 'Diabetes Mellitus' is characterized by? | High blood glucose levels |
| 10 | 'Photovoltaic cells' convert sunlight directly into? | Electricity |
| 11 | 'Nuclear fission' involves splitting of? | A heavy atomic nucleus |
| 12 | 'Nuclear fusion' involves combining of? | Light atomic nuclei |
| 13 | 'Isotopes' of an element differ in? | Number of neutrons |
| 14 | 'Radioactive decay' half-life refers to? | Time for half of a radioactive sample to decay |
| 15 | 'Doppler Effect' explains change in? | Frequency/pitch due to relative motion |
| 16 | 'Optical fibre' technology works on the principle of? | Total internal reflection |
| 17 | 'Satellite geostationary orbit' altitude (approx.)? | ~36,000 km above equator |
| 18 | 'Low Earth Orbit' (LEO) altitude range (approx.)? | 160-2,000 km |
| 19 | 'Reusable Launch Vehicle' technology aims to? | Reduce cost of space access by reusing rocket stages |
| 20 | 'GPS' relies on signals from how many satellites minimum for a 3D fix? | Four |
| 21 | 'Machine Learning' is best described as a subset of? | Artificial Intelligence |
| 22 | 'Deep Learning' relies primarily on? | Multi-layered neural networks |
| 23 | 'Internet of Things' (IoT) refers to? | Network of interconnected physical devices/sensors |
| 24 | '3D printing' is also known as? | Additive manufacturing |
| 25 | 'Biosensors' are devices that combine biological components with? | Electronic/physicochemical detectors |

## 64. Appendix AI: Indian Physiography & Biogeographic Zones

### AI.1 Major Physiographic Divisions of India

| Division | Sub-Division/Feature |
|----------|--------------------------|
| The Himalayan Mountains | Trans-Himalaya, Great Himalaya (Himadri), Middle Himalaya (Himachal), Shiwaliks (Outer Himalaya) |
| The Northern Plains | Bhabar, Tarai, Bhangar (older alluvium), Khadar (newer alluvium) |
| The Peninsular Plateau | Central Highlands, Deccan Plateau, Chotanagpur Plateau, Meghalaya Plateau |
| The Coastal Plains | Western Coastal Plain (Konkan, Malabar), Eastern Coastal Plain (Coromandel, Northern Circars) |
| The Indian Desert | Thar Desert (western Rajasthan) |
| The Islands | Andaman & Nicobar Islands (tectonic origin), Lakshadweep Islands (coral origin) |

### AI.2 Biogeographic Zones of India (Rodgers & Panwar Classification)

| Zone | Approx. Region |
|------|--------------------|
| Trans-Himalaya | Ladakh, high-altitude cold desert |
| Himalaya | Himalayan mountain ranges (Western, Central, Eastern Himalaya) |
| Desert | Thar Desert (Kachchh included in some classifications) |
| Semi-Arid | Parts of Rajasthan, Gujarat, Punjab, Haryana |
| Western Ghats | Western Ghats mountain range and adjoining areas |
| Deccan Plateau | Peninsular plateau region |
| Gangetic Plain | Indo-Gangetic plains |
| North-East India | Assam and adjoining north-eastern states |
| Coasts | Western and Eastern coastal stretches |
| Islands | Andaman & Nicobar and Lakshadweep archipelagos |
